You're a student learning to paint one subject beautifully, in the painterly pixel-art language of Mark Ferrari (with Studio Ghibli background painting as a kindred spirit): **Roosevelt elk and black-tailed deer, as individuals and as herds**.

Animals are how the world comes alive, and voxsim's animals are the weakest part of its pictures. Their bodies are rounded capsules that read as a little horsey. Elk and deer are in almost every scene. Learn to draw them so they're unmistakable and alive at every size, from a 60 px hero animal down to a 4 px dot in a herd seen from the air: silhouette, weight, gait, grazing, alert, fleeing, the rut, a calf at its mother's flank.

# Drawing animals: silhouette, posture, gait and group, at every distance

*Animal ecology, naturalists' studio, 2026-09-25. For the render lane and the students in `~/work/pixelart-studies` (see their `moodboards/quadrupeds.txt`: Liljefors, Rungius, Muybridge). This guide supplies the species truth: proportions, what the body does in each gait, which postures say which behaviour, and how groups arrange themselves. Measurements are standard mammalogy and my own reading of the photos in `animals/photos/` (±10–20%).*

## The principle: behaviour is legible in silhouette, and at a distance it is all you have

At 100 m an elk is a handful of pixels. What makes it an elk, and tells you whether it is grazing, alert or fleeing, is (1) the **ratio of body length to leg length to neck**, (2) the **head height relative to the back line**, (3) **the one high-contrast patch** (a pale rump, a white head, a black tail), and (4) **how it sits with the others** (spacing, facing, file). Get those four right and a 6-pixel elk reads. Liljefors is the model: "deer in landscape, small in frame yet unmistakable".

## 1. How big things look

Angular size in arcminutes ≈ **3.4 × size (m) / distance (km)**. The eye resolves ~1 arcminute.

| animal | body length × shoulder height (m) | voxels (0.5 m) | at 100 m | at 1 km |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Roosevelt elk | 2.4 × 1.5 (bull with antlers ~2.5 tall) | 5 × 3 | a clear figure, ~80' long | ~8': a dot with a pale rear, visible in the open |
| black-tailed deer | 1.5 × 0.95 | 3 × 2 | a small figure | ~5': visible only moving or on snow |
| black bear | 1.5 × 0.9 | 3 × 2 | a black lump | a black dot on green |
| cougar | 2.1 (incl. 0.8 tail) × 0.7 | 4 × 1.5 | long, low, the tail visible | practically invisible |
| coyote | 1.4 (incl. tail) × 0.55 | 3 × 1 | a thin, grey dog shape | invisible |
| mountain goat | 1.4 × 1.0 | 3 × 2 | a white block | **a white dot on a dark cliff** |
...

# What earlier students discovered: notes on their craft

These are notes on the painstaking work of the students who came before you:

- four who each spent hours learning Ferrari's language on one small scene (`pedagogy/{atelier,master-copy,analysis,observation}`);
- seven who studied single topics.

They copied his pixels crop by crop, measured his grass against real grass, built a boulder up from a lit sphere, and diagrammed his picture before daring to paint it. What they found is here to inspire you and save you time. It isn't a specification. You'll see things they didn't, and some of what they wrote will turn out wrong or incomplete for your subject. Follow your own eyes. Their best work is in their intermediate studies (e.g. `master-copy/out/cmp_c*.png`, `atelier/stage2/`, `observation/studies/translate_*.png`, `analysis/img/`); those are worth looking at more than their final painters.

The tags below say how each finding came about:

- **[shown]**: a student built it and it worked.
- **[measured]**: a student measured it in Ferrari's pixels or in photos.
- **[mine]**: the integrator's reading, not yet tried by anyone.

Striking to us: the four students, working apart, found much the same underlying ideas.

## The convergence

Working alone, all four arms landed on the same architecture:

- **Paint indices, not colours.**
  - Master copy found that Ferrari's index map is literally a form map: indices are bins of surface direction, and "the palette is the light". The same index image serves morning and afternoon. [measured]
  - Atelier paints in "step space" along one material ramp. [shown]
  - Sky found that two of Ferrari's hour pairs are identical pixels. [measured]
  - Analysis found that value is the structure and colour is temperature and distance. [measured]
- **Two light families that never touch.** Atelier's terminator rule [shown], analysis's three-value picture plan with every mass kept inside its group [measured], and rock's lit and shadow groups [shown] are the same idea at two scales.
- **Dither mixes only adjacent steps of one ramp.** All four arms use it. Ordered 2×2 dither suits hard or precise things (rock, sky seams, water rows); random or diffusion dither suits organic texture (lawns, foam). Master copy measured this [measured]; analysis cites Ferrari's GDC talk for the same split.
- **The medium's own geometry.** Contours are clean runs (1:1, 2:1, 4-4-4 flats), measured on Ferrari's crests by analysis and atelier [measured]. Atelier redraws every major contour by snapping edges to clean ratios [shown]. Masses meet level ground or water in a dead-flat run with a 1-px darker contact line [measured: analysis, observation].
- **Clean runs are for narrow forms; mass boundaries are where texture ends.** David's correction (2026-09-25): the clean-run rule came from the *grass strokes*. It applies to anything narrow, drawn as a line: blades, cracks, joints, twigs, conifer spires, stems, a snowline drawn as a stroke. A 1–2 px fo

# Willow scrub, after Mark Ferrari: lessons

A student's log. Subject: the low many-stemmed shrubby willows of braided rivers and gravel bars.
Code in this folder: `wpaint.py` (index canvas, clean runs, sheets), `bush.py` (form: wands, lobes,
value plan), `foliage.py` (the marks: clump grammar, speckle, leaf glyphs, fringe, wand tips),
`willow.py` (the painter), `lw.py` + `copyharness.py` + `stage0_copy.py` (master copies),
`transfer.py` (copy -> willow steps), one script per later stage.

Photos: `refs/` (fetched with the studio's imgfetch; `refs/contact.jpg` is the contact sheet). The ones
I lean on: `crop_nzbush.png` (a single low willow on tussock, NZ), the Rio Grande band
(`refs/more/openverse_Rio_Grande_in_White_Rock_Canyon_*`), the Alaskan gold willow flats
(`refs/more/openverse_Be_Careful_*`), Salix triandra on a river bank, and the two in `transfer/refs`.

## Stage 0: copy Ferrari first, so the technique has a proven target

Ferrari never painted willows, so I copied the passages of his closest to them: leafy masses in
*Living Worlds* "Deep Forest" (V09), read from the original index maps (`lw.py`).

- **C1**, the sunlit foreground bush, native (0,322)-(140,380): near-scale leaves.
- **C2**, the middle-distance hedge band behind the pool, native (265,222)-(395,282): the closest
  thing in his work to a band of scrub along water, with its crest against the sky and stems in
  the dark below.

The copy uses his palette and only the copyist's sketch (which ramp owns each region, and a blurred
light field, as the master-copy study did). Everything at pixel level is generated.

### What reading his indices showed

- **Ramps are per plant, stacked vertically inside a leaf.** In C1 there are three little ramps
  (lime, olive, dim green) that share one near-black. Down a column, a lit pixel sits on the next
  step of its own ramp (lime 9 is above green 10 39% of the time), then darker, then black: every
  leaf is lit on its upper edge and darkens downward.
- **Lit components are tiny.** 46% of the brightest-step components are single pixels, 9% vertical
  pairs, 9% horizontal pairs. A leaf is a composite of a lit top row (1–4 px) over a mid row, with
  1-px stems (vertical runs of the mid step, 3–6 px) among them.
- **The middle distance is bimodal.** In C2 the base is two close darks (L 59/79) and the marks two
  lights (L 131/175); the middle step (L 103) is only 8% of pixels. Contrast lives inside the mark,
  the mass stays calm.
- **The far hedge (C ramp) is lighter, bluer and flatter**, mostly the two middle steps in checker.

### The technique, as discovered (all in `foliage.py`)

1. **The clump grammar** (`clump_foliage`), which the critic pointed me to and which is the big one.
   Foliage is a heap of clumps (C1: 5–12 px; C2: 4–13 px), painted top to bottom so lower clumps lie
   in front. Each clump has its own light: a lit cap toward the sun, a body, a dark band along its
   bottom that separates it from the clump below. The clumps

Let me look at the moodboard, the willow best images, and the naturalist photos.

//! Animal sheet on a flat meadow (no sim): every quadruped species, adult
//! (male and female), young, standing / grazing / walking, at scene scales.
//!
//!   cargo run --release -p vox-paint --example animals -- OUT_DIR
use vox_core::*;
use vox_paint::*;

fn main() {
    let out = std::path::PathBuf::from(std::env::args().nth(1).unwrap_or_else(|| "renders/paint/animals".into()));
    std::fs::create_dir_all(&out).unwrap();
    let size = IVec3::new(128, 128, 32);
    let mut w = World::new(size);
    w.sea_level = 2;
    for y in 0..size.y {
        for x in 0..size.x {
            for z in 0..=8 {
                let m = if z < 7 { Material::Subsoil } else { Material::Topsoil };
                let mut v = Voxel::of(m);
                v.moisture = 120;
                w.set(IVec3::new(x, y, z), v);
            }
            if (x * 7 + y * 13) % 3 != 0 {
                w.set(IVec3::new(x, y, 9), Voxel::of(Material::Grass));
            }
        }
    }
    let species = ["red deer", "elk", "ibex", "wild sheep", "wild boar", "wolf", "fox", "brown bear", "lynx", "hare", "beaver", "squirrel"];
    let mut agents = AgentTable::default();
    let gz = 9.0;
    // Rows (screen): species; columns: male, female, young, grazing, walking.
    for (i, k) in species.iter().enumerate() {
        for j in 0..5 {
            // Place along the screen axes for yaw 45 (rotation 0): screen
            // right = (+x, -y), down = (+x, +y).
            let (r, d) = (-14.0 + j as f32 * 7.0, 24.0 - i as f32 * 4.4);
            let (x, y) = (64.0 + (r + d) * 0.7071, 64.0 + (d - r) * 0.7071);
            let mut tags = Tags::ANIMAL;
            if j == 0 || j == 4 {
                tags = tags | Tags::MALE;
            }
            if j == 2 {
                tags = tags | Tags::YOUNG;
            }
            let id = agents.spawn(SubsystemId(0), AgentSpec { kind: k, name: None, pos: Vec3::new(x, y, gz + 1.0), extent: 2.0, label: k.to_string(), tags, color: [120, 90, 60] }, 0);
            let a = agents.get_mut(id).unwrap();
            // Facing screen right (east-south-east) or left.
            a.heading = if j % 2 == 0 { -0.785 } else { 2.356 };
            let desc = match j {
                3 => "graze on the grass",
                4 => "walk to the river",
                _ => "stand watch",
            };
            a.intent = Some(Intent { action: Action::new("idle", Target::None), desc: desc.into(), started_tick: 0 });
        }
    }
    let clock = Clock::default();
    let env = Env::default();
    let mut p = Painter::new();
    for mpp in [0.05f32, 0.075, 0.12] {
        let mut v = View::new(64.0, 64.0, Scale::Scene);
        v.ppm = 1.0 / mpp;
        v.zf = auto_zf(v.ppm);
        v.hour = Some(16.5);
        v.upscale = 3;
        v.z_center = Some((gz + 1.0) * VOXEL_M);
        let t = std::time::Instant::now();
        let img = p.paint(&w, &agents, &clock, &env, &v);
        let f = out.join(format!("animals_{mpp:.3}.png"));

# Driftwood, after Mark Ferrari

Whole trees and logs stranded on river gravel bars: silvered, barkless trunks with root wads and
snapped stubs, piled against each other. Everything here is made by the programs in `src/`; the
stage images are in `stages/`, the parameter sheets in `sheets/`. Photos are in `refs/` (sources
in `refs/sources.tsv`) plus `~/work/pixelart-studies/transfer/refs/braided_driftwood_bar.jpg`.



src/run_all.sh                                # every stage image and sheet (~9 min, 4 processes)
nice -n 10 python3 src/driftwood.py sheets    # just the sheets




The technique code:

- `src/geo.py` is the world: logs as bent, tapering round-cone chains, ends, knots, stubs, root
  fans, sphere tracing and cast shadows.
- `src/tech.py` is the cylinder form rule proven on Ferrari's trunk.
- `src/render.py` is the painter: classes, outline, ends, contact, separation, root mass, wet
  glints, strokes.
- `src/marks.py` holds grain, checks, knots and bark.
- `src/gravel.py` is the ground.
- `src/palette.py` is the light (hour and wetness).
- `src/pile.py` places the logs of a pile.
- `src/driftwood.py` is the single `paint(...)` call and the sheets.

---

## Stage 0: copying Ferrari first

Ferrari never painted driftwood. The nearest passages in his work are the pale sunlit trunk in
*Deep Forest* (V09), its grey barked trunk, and the fallen dead tree in the dusk marsh of V02.
I read his indexed files directly (the Canvas Cycle JSON; same loader as the master-copy arm), so
I could see his form classes as numbers rather than guessing them from RGB.

### 0A: the pale trunk (V09), a smooth cylinder in sun

![s0a](stages/s0a_pale_trunk_8x.png)
![s0a2](stages/s0a2_pipeline_copy_8x.png)

Printed as index symbols, the 13-px trunk is **five form classes in a fixed spatial order**,
counted from the shadow edge:



cc 4444 3 3 1 1 2 2        c = cool sky-blue rim   4 = core   3 = halftone   1 = light   2 = lit edge




- In value those are 138 | 125 | 149 | 179 | 166: reflected rim, core, halftone, light, and a lit
  edge that falls back one step as the form turns away. The classical order, and the whole range
  is only 55 grey levels: the mass is held close.
- Between classes he uses a checker of the two neighbours, 1–2 px wide, running along the axis.
  Only three mixing levels occur: pure, 50%, pure. The pattern repeats every 8 rows exactly
  (a DPaint gradient fill).
- **Dithering follows spatial adjacency, not value adjacency.** The core (125) dithers straight
  into the halftone (149), skipping the cool rim colour (138) that lies between them in value.

The first copy (`s0a`) fitted a 1-D profile across his own silhouette: 87% of trunk pixels match
his class exactly. The second (`s0a2`) matters more: it paints the trunk from a lit 3-D cylinder,
with the classes as a function of the angle `d` around the axis measured from

"""Load Ferrari's Living Worlds scenes as index maps (format from the Canvas Cycle demo)."""
import re, json, numpy as np
REF = "~/work/pixelart-studies/pedagogy/master-copy/ref/lw"
def load(name):
    t = open(f"{REF}/{name}.json").read()
    t = re.sub(r'([{,])\s*([A-Za-z_]+)\s*:', r'\1"\2":', t).replace("'", '"')
    d = json.loads(t)
    d["pal"] = np.array(d["colors"], dtype=np.uint8)
    d["idx"] = np.array(d["pixels"], dtype=np.int32).reshape(d["height"], d["width"])
    return d

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""imgfetch: fast, polite reference-image sourcing (Wikimedia Commons + Openverse).

  python3 imgfetch.py "cinder cone scoria slope" -n 12 -o refs/cinder
  python3 imgfetch.py -f queries.txt -n 8 -o refs            # one query per line; subdir per query
  options: --src commons,openverse   --width 1200   --sheet (writes <out>/_sheet.jpg)

Writes images plus <out>/sources.tsv (file, title, license, author, page url).
Cached: files already present are skipped. Handles 429/5xx with Retry-After backoff.
Parallel downloads (4 workers) with a shared per-host rate limiter (~3 req/s; Wikimedia's media
host is kept to 1 per 2 s and slows further on any 429, since it throttles hard per IP after bursts).
Openverse (mostly Flickr-hosted CC images) is the primary source because it stays fast.
"""
import argparse, concurrent.futures as cf, json, os, re, sys, threading, time, urllib.error, urllib.parse, urllib.request

UA = "pixelart-studies-imgfetch/1.0 (research; contact: local)"
_locks = {}; _last = {}; _glock = threading.Lock()

SLOW_HOSTS = {"upload.wikimedia.org": 2.0}   # Wikimedia media host: ~1 req/s or it 429s

def _throttle(host, min_gap=0.34):
    min_gap = SLOW_HOSTS.get(host, min_gap)
    with _glock:
        lk = _locks.setdefault(host, threading.Lock())
    with lk:
        dt = time.time() - _last.get(host, 0)
        if dt < min_gap:
            time.sleep(min_gap - dt)
        _last[host] = time.time()

def get(url, tries=4, timeout=25):
    host = urllib.parse.urlparse(url).netloc
    if host == "upload.wikimedia.org":
        tries = 2  # fail fast; other sources fill in
    for i in range(tries):
        _throttle(host)
        try:
            req = urllib.request.Request(url, headers={"User-Agent": UA})
            return urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=timeout).read()
        except urllib.error.HTTPError as e:

# output:
"""Willow palettes: (material, step) -> RGB for a season, an hour and a depth.

Light as hue shift: every ramp walks from a cool dark (tinted by the sky's ambient colour) to a warm
light (tinted by the sun). Depth lifts and cools the darks and leaves the lights nearly alone
(measured on Ferrari by the master-copy study). Silver is its own ramp: the undersides of willow
leaves, grey-green to pale silver, cooler than the green.
"""
import numpy as np
from wpaint import SKY, GRAVEL, LEAF, SILVER, STEM, WATER, GRASS, EARTH, FAR

LEAFS = 10


def _lin(c):
    c = np.asarray(c, float) / 255
    return np.where(c > 0.04045, ((c + 0.055) / 1.055) ** 2.4, c / 12.92)


def _srgb(l):
    l = np.clip(l, 0, 1)
    return np.where(l > 0.0031308, 1.055 * l ** (1 / 2.4) - 0.055, 12.92 * l) * 255


def to_lab(c):
    l = _lin(c)
    M1 = np.array([[0.4122214708, 0.5363325363, 0.0514459929], [0.2119034982, 0.6806995451, 0.1073969566],
                   [0.0883024619, 0.2817188376, 0.6299787005]])
    lms = np.cbrt(M1 @ l)
    M2 = np.array([[0.2104542553, 0.7936177850, -0.0040720468], [1.9779984951, -2.4285922050, 0.4505937099],
                   [0.0259040371, 0.7827717662, -0.8086757660]])
    return M2 @ lms


def to_rgb(lab):
    M2i = np.array([[1, 0.3963377774, 0.2158037573], [1, -0.1055613458, -0.0638541728], [1, -0.0894841775, -1.2914855480]])
    lms = (M2i @ lab) ** 3
    M1i = np.array([[4.0767416621, -3.3077115913, 0.2309699292], [-1.2684380046, 2.6097574011, -0.3413193965],
                    [-0.0041960863, -0.7034186147, 1.7076147010]])
    return tuple(int(round(v)) for v in _srgb(M1i @ lms))


def mix(a, b, t):
    return to_rgb(to_lab(a) * (1 - t) + to_lab(b) * t)


def ramp(dark, light, n, sun, amb, warm=0.25, cool=0.3):
    """n steps from dark to light in OKLab; lights pulled toward the sun colour, darks toward ambient."""
    out = []
    for i in range(n):
        t = i / max(n - 1, 1)
        c = to_lab(dark) * (1 - t) + to_lab(light) * t
        c = c * (1 - warm * t ** 1.5) + to_lab(sun) * warm * t ** 1.5 if warm else c
        c = c * (1 - cool * (1 - t) ** 2) + to_lab(amb) * cool * (1 - t) ** 2 if cool else c
        out.append(to_rgb(c))
    return out


SEASONS = {
    #          lit base (dark, light)          lit leaves (mid, top)          silver (lo, hi)            twig (dark, light)       shadow (dark, mark)
    "summer": dict(base=((14, 36, 18), (30, 62, 30)), leaf=((92, 132, 56), (168, 200, 96)), silver=((120, 142, 128), (196, 214, 200)),
palette.py:14:def _lin(c):
palette.py:19:def _srgb(l):
palette.py:24:def to_lab(c):
palette.py:34:def to_rgb(lab):
palette.py:42:def mix(a, b, t):
palette.py:46:def ramp(dark, light, n, sun, amb, warm=0.25, cool=0.3):
palette.py:80:def willow_palette(season="summer", hour="noon", depth=0.0, haze=None):
wpaint.py:25:def grey_palette():
wpaint.py:73:def norm3(v):
wpaint.py:78:def sun(az_deg, el_deg):
wpaint.py:84:def value_noise(shape, scale, rng, octaves=1):
wpaint.py:101:def upscale(img, k):
Write: elements/deer-elk/src/rig.py
"""The animal as a 3-D rig: species proportions, pose, gait -> a list of tubes.

Units are shoulder heights (H = 1). Axes: x forward, y left, z up; the animal stands on z = 0
with its withers near x = 0.3.

A tube is a centreline of stations, each with an elliptical cross-section (a along the local
"up" normal, b along the lateral binormal), a material function, and a flag for thin parts
(lower legs, ears, antlers, tail) that the pixel painter redraws as clean runs when they get
narrower than a pixel or two.

The torso is not a capsule. It is lofted from stations read off side-view photos and the
Muybridge plates: the level back with the withers a touch above the croup, the deep chest, the
belly rising into the flank tuck, the ham dropping behind it and the buttock. That profile is
most of what separates a cervid from the horse the old capsule rig drew.
"""
import numpy as np
from dataclasses import dataclass, field, replace

# material ids (hide regions). The painter maps each to a ramp.
BODY, DARK, PALE, BELLY, LEG, HOOF, ANTLER, TIP, NOSE, EARIN, THROAT, TAILTOP, SPOT, VELVET, EYE = range(15)
MAT_NAMES = ["body", "dark", "pale", "belly", "leg", "hoof", "antler", "tip", "nose", "earin",
             "throat", "tailtop", "spot", "velvet", "eye"]


@dataclass
class Tube:
    name: str
    c: np.ndarray        # (n,3) centreline
    a: np.ndarray        # (n,) radius along up-normal
    b: np.ndarray        # (n,) radius along lateral
    up: np.ndarray       # (3,) reference up for framing
    mat: object          # int or fn(u in 0..1 along, theta, pts) -> ids
    thin: bool = False
    cap0: bool = True
    cap1: bool = True
    width_px_min: float = 1.0   # when drawn as a line, never thinner than this


# ---------------------------------------------------------------- species

@dataclass
class Species:
    name: str
    H: float                     # shoulder height in metres
    torso: list                  # stations (x, ztop, zbot, halfwidth)
    neck_base: tuple             # (x, z) where the neck leaves the chest
    neck_len: float
    neck_r0: tuple               # (a, b) at base
    neck_r1: tuple               # (a, b) at the head
    head_len: float
    head_r0: tuple               # cranium (a, b)
    head_r1: tuple               # muzzle (a, b)
    ear_len: float
    ear_w: float
    fore_attach: tuple           # (x, z, y) elbow
    hind_attach: tuple           # (x, z, y) stifle
    fore_len: tuple              # forearm, cannon, pastern+hoof
    hind_len: tuple              # gaskin, cannon, pastern+hoof
    fore_r: tuple                # forearm top, carpus, cannon, fetlock, hoof
    hind_r: tuple                # gaskin top, hock, cannon, fetlock, hoof
    tail_len: float
    tail_w: float
    mane: float = 0.0            # extra ventral depth of the neck (bull mane), in H
    colour: str = "elk"


ELK_TORSO = [
    # x      ztop   zbot   hw        (from side-view photos of Roosevelt cows, Muybridge 692)
    (0.60, 0.79, 0.68, 0.05),
    (0.54, 0.90, 0.58, 0.12),
    (0.44, 0.985, 0.52, 0.16),   # chest, deep
    (0.30, 1.00, 0.50, 0.18),    # withers peak over the girth
    (0.12, 0.985, 0.51, 0.20),
    (-0.08, 0.965, 0.53, 0.21),  # barrel
    (-0.28, 0.955, 0.575, 0.20),
    (-0.42, 0.955, 0.62, 0.18),  # flank tuck
    (-0.54, 0.96, 0.60, 0.175),  # ham drops behind the tuck
    (-0.66, 0.945, 0.62, 0.15),
    (-0.75, 0.90, 0.67, 0.10),   # buttock
    (-0.80, 0.83, 0.72, 0.04),
]

DEER_TORSO = [
    (0.55, 0.78, 0.66, 0.045),
    (0.49, 0.89, 0.57, 0.11),
    (0.40, 0.975, 0.52, 0.145),
    (0.27, 1.00, 0.51, 0.16),
    (0.10, 0.985, 0.52, 0.175),
    (-0.08, 0.97, 0.54, 0.18),
    (-0.26, 0.97, 0.585, 0.17),
    (-0.40, 0.975, 0.63, 0.155),
    (-0.51, 0.985, 0.61, 0.15),  # deer rump sits level with or above the withers
    (-0.62, 0.975, 0.63, 0.13),
    (-0.70, 0.93, 0.68, 0.085),
    (-0.74, 0.87, 0.73, 0.035),
]

SPECIES = {
    "elk": Species(
        name="elk", H=1.45, torso=ELK_TORSO,
        neck_base=(0.46, 0.80), neck_len=0.56, neck_r0=(0.19, 0.12), neck_r1=(0.10, 0.075),
        head_len=0.40, head_r0=(0.085, 0.075), head_r1=(0.05, 0.042),
        ear_len=0.15, ear_w=0.055,
        fore_attach=(0.36, 0.60, 0.105), hind_attach=(-0.50, 0.70, 0.11),
        fore_len=(0.30, 0.235, 0.085), hind_len=(0.33, 0.30, 0.085),
        fore_r=(0.068, 0.036, 0.026, 0.032, 0.03), hind_r=(0.085, 0.04, 0.027, 0.032, 0.03),
        tail_len=0.09, tail_w=0.04, colour="elk"),
    "deer": Species(
        name="deer", H=0.92, torso=DEER_TORSO,
        neck_base=(0.43, 0.80), neck_len=0.52, neck_r0=(0.15, 0.10), neck_r1=(0.075, 0.06),
        head_len=0.33, head_r0=(0.075, 0.068), head_r1=(0.038, 0.034),
        ear_len=0.21, ear_w=0.085,
        fore_attach=(0.33, 0.60, 0.09), hind_attach=(-0.46, 0.71, 0.095),
        fore_len=(0.30, 0.25, 0.08), hind_len=(0.33, 0.32, 0.08),
        fore_r=(0.055, 0.03, 0.02, 0.026, 0.024), hind_r=(0.075, 0.034, 0.021, 0.026, 0.024),
        tail_len=0.20, tail_w=0.06, colour="deer"),
}


@dataclass
class Individual:
    species: str = "elk"
    sex: str = "f"               # f, m
    age: str = "adult"           # adult, yearling, young (calf / fawn)
    season: str = "autumn"       # spring, summer, autumn, winter (coat, antler state)
    seed: int = 0

    def spec(self):
        sp = SPECIES[self.species]
        rng = np.random.default_rng(self.seed + 17)
        j = lambda s: 1 + rng.normal(0, s)
        sp = replace(sp)
        if self.sex == "m" and self.age == "adult":
            if self.species == "elk":
                # bulls: bigger front end, heavier neck, the mane
                sp = replace(sp, neck_r0=(0.24, 0.14), neck_r1=(0.12, 0.085), mane=0.07,
                             head_r0=(0.095, 0.082), torso=[(x, zt + (0.02 if x > 0 else 0), zb - (0.015 if x > 0.1 else 0), hw * 1.05) for x, zt, zb, hw in sp.torso])
            else:
                sp = replace(sp, neck_r0=(0.17, 0.11), neck_r1=(0.085, 0.066))
        if self.age == "young":
            # calves and fawns: legs long for the body, big head and ears, short neck
            s = 0.62
            sp = replace(sp, torso=[(x * 0.9, 0.62 + (zt - 0.62) * 0.8, 0.62 + (zb - 0.62) * 0.8 + 0.03, hw * 0.8) for x, zt, zb, hw in sp.torso],
                         neck_len=sp.neck_len * 0.8, neck_r0=tuple(v * 0.8 for v in sp.neck_r0), neck_r1=tuple(v * 0.85 for v in sp.neck_r1),
                         head_len=sp.head_len * 0.82, head_r0=tuple(v * 0.9 for v in sp.head_r0), head_r1=tuple(v * 0.9 for v in sp.head_r1),
                         ear_len=sp.ear_len * 1.05, ear_w=sp.ear_w * 1.05,
                         fore_attach=(sp.fore_attach[0] * 0.9, 0.62 + (sp.fore_attach[1] - 0.62) * 0.8 + 0.035, sp.fore_attach[2] * 0.8),
                         hind_attach=(sp.hind_attach[0] * 0.9, 0.62 + (sp.hind_attach[1] - 0.62) * 0.8 + 0.035, sp.hind_attach[2] * 0.8),
                         neck_base=(sp.neck_base[0] * 0.9, 0.62 + (sp.neck_base[1] - 0.62) * 0.8 + 0.03),
                         fore_len=tuple(v * 1.06 for v in sp.fore_len), hind_len=tuple(v * 1.06 for v in sp.hind_len),
                         fore_r=tuple(v * 0.8 for v in sp.fore_r), hind_r=tuple(v * 0.8 for v in sp.hind_r),
                         H=sp.H * s)
        elif self.age == "yearling":
            sp = replace(sp, H=sp.H * 0.88, torso=[(x, zt, zb + 0.01, hw * 0.92) for x, zt, zb, hw in sp.torso])
        # individual variation, a few percent
        sp = replace(sp, H=sp.H * j(0.03), neck_len=sp.neck_len * j(0.03), head_len=sp.head_len * j(0.03),
                     torso=[(x * j(0.004), zt, zb, hw) for x, zt, zb, hw in sp.torso])
        return sp

    def antler(self):
        """None, 'spike', 'velvet', 'hard' (+ 'deer' variants)."""
        if self.sex != "m":
            return None
        if self.age == "young":
            return None
        if self.season == "spring":
            return None if self.species == "elk" else None
        if self.season == "summer":
            return "velvet"
        if self.age == "yearling":
            return "spike"
        return "hard"


# ---------------------------------------------------------------- pose

@dataclass
class Pose:
    """Every angle in degrees. Legs are either IK foot targets (gait) or explicit angles (lie)."""
    body_z: float = 0.0          # vertical offset of the whole body
    pitch: float = 0.0           # nose up +
    flex: float = 0.0            # spine flexion: + arches (gathered), - hollows (extended)
    neck_ang: float = 42.0       # neck axis above horizontal
    head_ang: float = -48.0      # head axis relative to horizontal (negative = nose down)
    neck_turn: float = 0.0       # yaw of the neck (toward the viewer +)
    ear_ang: float = 25.0        # ears up from horizontal-back
    ear_fwd: float = 0.0         # ears cupped forward (alert) 0..1
    tail_up: float = 0.0         # 0 down, 1 flagged
    mouth: float = 0.0           # bugle
    legs: dict = field(default_factory=dict)   # name -> ("ik", (x, z), pastern_deg) or ("fk", (a1, a2, a3))
    lie: float = 0.0             # 0 standing, 1 bedded


LEG_NAMES = ["LF", "RF", "LH", "RH"]


def gait_pose(sp: Species, gait: str, phase: float, rng=None) -> Pose:
    """A pose from a gait and a phase in [0,1). Timings after Muybridge (walk: lateral sequence;
    trot: diagonals; gallop: transverse, gathered suspension; bound and stot for deer)."""
    p = Pose()
    fx, fz, _ = sp.fore_attach
    hx, hz, _ = sp.hind_attach
    stand_h_f = sum(sp.fore_len) * 0.985
    if gait == "stand":
        p.legs = {"LF": ("ik", (fx + 0.02, 0.0), 38), "RF": ("ik", (fx - 0.03, 0.0), 38),
                  "LH": ("ik", (hx - 0.02, 0.0), 44), "RH": ("ik", (hx + 0.04, 0.0), 44)}
        return p
    if gait == "alert":
        p.neck_ang, p.head_ang, p.ear_fwd = 72.0, -18.0, 1.0
        p.legs = {"LF": ("ik", (fx + 0.04, 0.0), 36), "RF": ("ik", (fx - 0.0, 0.0), 36),
                  "LH": ("ik", (hx - 0.04, 0.0), 44), "RH": ("ik", (hx + 0.0, 0.0), 44)}
        return p
    if gait == "graze":
        p.neck_ang, p.head_ang, p.ear_ang = -52.0, -82.0, 10.0
        p.pitch = -1.5
        p.legs = {"LF": ("ik", (fx + 0.10, 0.0), 40), "RF": ("ik", (fx - 0.02, 0.0), 40),
                  "LH": ("ik", (hx - 0.03, 0.0), 44), "RH": ("ik", (hx + 0.06, 0.0), 44)}
        return p
    if gait == "bugle":
        p.neck_ang, p.head_ang, p.mouth, p.ear_ang = 32.0, 38.0, 1.0, -10.0
        p.legs = {"LF": ("ik", (fx + 0.07, 0.0), 36), "RF": ("ik", (fx + 0.0, 0.0), 36),
                  "LH": ("ik", (hx - 0.06, 0.0), 44), "RH": ("ik", (hx - 0.01, 0.0), 44)}
        return p
    if gait == "lie":
        p.lie = 1.0
        p.body_z = -(fz - 0.02) - 0.02
        p.neck_ang, p.head_ang = 55.0, -30.0
        p.legs = {"LF": ("fk", (-100, 70, -40)), "RF": ("fk", (-95, 160, 40)),
                  "LH": ("fk", (80, -150, 30)), "RH": ("fk", (75, -145, 20))}
        return p

    # moving gaits: (offsets per leg, duty factor, stride, lift fore, lift hind, bob)
    G = {
        "walk":   dict(off={"LH": 0.0, "LF": 0.25, "RH": 0.5, "RF": 0.75}, duty=0.66, stride=0.52, lf=0.13, lh=0.10, bob=0.012,
                       neck=30.0, head=-45.0),
        "trot":   dict(off={"LF": 0.0, "RH": 0.0, "RF": 0.5, "LH": 0.5}, duty=0.42, stride=0.78, lf=0.30, lh=0.18, bob=0.03,
                       neck=48.0, head=-22.0),
        "gallop": dict(off={"LH": 0.0, "RH": 0.08, "LF": 0.44, "RF": 0.54}, duty=0.30, stride=1.05, lf=0.26, lh=0.22, bob=0.07,
                       neck=24.0, head=-30.0),
        "bound":  dict(off={"LH": 0.0, "RH": 0.02, "LF": 0.40, "RF": 0.44}, duty=0.26, stride=1.1, lf=0.34, lh=0.30, bob=0.12,
                       neck=40.0, head=-25.0),
        "stot":   dict(off={"LH": 0.0, "RH": 0.0, "LF": 0.0, "RF": 0.0}, duty=0.25, stride=0.25, lf=0.35, lh=0.35, bob=0.25,
                       neck=50.0, head=-20.0),
    }[gait]
    p.neck_ang, p.head_ang = G["neck"], G["head"]
    legs = {}
    airborne_all = True
    for name in LEG_NAMES:
        fore = name[1] == "F"
        ph = (phase - G["off"][name]) % 1.0
        ax = fx if fore else hx
        S = G["stride"]
        # foot travels relative to its limb root: stance +S/2 -> -S/2 on the ground, then swing
        if ph < G["duty"]:
            s = ph / G["duty"]
            x = ax + S * (0.5 - s) + (0.02 if fore else -0.0)
            z = 0.0
            past = 42 if fore else 46
            if gait in ("gallop", "bound"):
                past = 30 + 25 * np.sin(np.pi * s)
            airborne_all = False
        else:
            s = (ph - G["duty"]) / (1 - G["duty"])
            e = 0.5 - 0.5 * np.cos(np.pi * s)            # ease
            x = ax + S * (-0.5 + e)
            lift = G["lf"] if fore else G["lh"]
            z = lift * np.sin(np.pi * s ** (0.8 if fore else 1.1))
            # swing: the pastern folds (fore: hoof turns up behind; hind: tucked)
            past = (-70 if fore else -40) * np.sin(np.pi * s) + (42 if fore else 46) * (1 - np.sin(np.pi * s))
            if gait == "stot":
                x = ax + 0.02
                z = 0.0
                past = 20
        legs[name] = ("ik", (x, z), past)
    p.legs = legs
    # body bob: lowest mid-stance of the diagonal, highest at suspension
    if gait == "walk":
        p.body_z = -G["bob"] * np.cos(4 * np.pi * phase)
        p.head_ang += 6 * np.sin(4 * np.pi * phase)
        p.neck_ang += 4 * np.sin(4 * np.pi * phase + 0.6)
    elif gait == "trot":
        p.body_z = G["bob"] * np.cos(4 * np.pi * phase)
    elif gait in ("gallop", "bound"):
        # gathered suspension around phase ~0.85 (all four under), extended around 0.35
        p.body_z = G["bob"] * (0.5 + 0.5 * np.cos(2 * np.pi * (phase - 0.82)))
        p.flex = 9 * np.cos(2 * np.pi * (phase - 0.82))
        p.pitch = 6 * np.sin(2 * np.pi * (phase - 0.2))
        p.neck_ang += 8 * np.sin(2 * np.pi * (phase - 0.1))
        p.tail_up = 1.0
    elif gait == "stot":
        up = max(0.0, np.sin(2 * np.pi * phase))
        p.body_z = G["bob"] * up
        p.tail_up = 1.0
        for name in LEG_NAMES:
            fore = name[1] == "F"
            p.legs[name] = ("ik", ((fx + 0.03) if fore else (hx - 0.02), 0.0 if up == 0 else 0.0), 25)
    if gait in ("gallop", "bound"):
        p.ear_ang = -5.0
    return p


# ---------------------------------------------------------------- geometry helpers

def _rot_y(v, deg, about):
    """Rotate points in the x-z plane (pitch) about a point."""
    t = np.radians(deg)
    c, s = np.cos(t), np.sin(t)
    v = np.asarray(v, float) - about
    x, z = v[..., 0], v[..., 2]
    out = v.copy()
    out[..., 0] = c * x - s * z
    out[..., 2] = s * x + c * z
    return out + about


def _dir(ang_deg, turn_deg=0.0):
    t = np.radians(ang_deg)
    u = np.radians(turn_deg)
    return np.array([np.cos(t) * np.cos(u), np.cos(t) * np.sin(u), np.sin(t)])


def ik2(root, target, L1, L2, bend_forward):
    """2-bone IK in the x-z plane. Returns the joint. bend_forward: joint lies ahead (+x) of the
    root-target line."""
    root = np.asarray(root, float)
    target = np.asarray(target, float)
    d = target - root
    D = np.linalg.norm(d)
    D = np.clip(D, abs(L1 - L2) + 1e-4, L1 + L2 - 1e-4)
    d = d / (np.linalg.norm(d) + 1e-9)
    a = (L1 * L1 - L2 * L2 + D * D) / (2 * D)
    h = np.sqrt(max(L1 * L1 - a * a, 0.0))
    perp = np.array([-d[1], d[0]])       # left-normal in (x, z)
    if (perp[0] > 0) != bend_forward:
        perp = -perp
    return root + d * a + perp * h


def loft(stations, n=24):
    """Resample torso stations to n stations with a smooth (Catmull-Rom-ish) interpolation."""
    st = np.array(stations, float)
    xs = st[:, 0]
    order = np.argsort(xs)
    st = st[order]
    xx = np.linspace(st[0, 0], st[-1, 0], n)
    from scipy.interpolate import PchipInterpolator
    out = [xx]
    for k in range(1, 4):
        out.append(PchipInterpolator(st[:, 0], st[:, k])(xx))
    return np.stack(out, 1)


def tube_from_points(name, pts, ra, rb, up, mat, thin=False, n=None, cap0=True, cap1=True):
    pts = np.asarray(pts, float)
    ra = np.broadcast_to(np.asarray(ra, float), (len(pts),)).copy()
    rb = np.broadcast_to(np.asarray(rb, float), (len(pts),)).copy()
    if n is not None and n != len(pts):
        # resample by arclength
        seg = np.linalg.norm(np.diff(pts, axis=0), axis=1)
        s = np.concatenate([[0], np.cumsum(seg)])
        t = np.linspace(0, s[-1], n)
        pts = np.stack([np.interp(t, s, pts[:, k]) for k in range(3)], 1)
        ra = np.interp(t, s, ra)
        rb = np.interp(t, s, rb)
    return Tube(name, pts, ra, rb, np.asarray(up, float), mat, thin, cap0, cap1)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------- build

def build(ind: Individual, pose: Pose):
    """-> list of Tube, in metres, in the animal's frame (x forward, z up, ground z = 0)."""
    sp = ind.spec()
    tubes = []
    H = sp.H
    young = ind.age == "young"

    # body transform: flex (front and back halves pitch opposite ways about the girth), pitch, body_z
    pivot = np.array([0.0, 0.0, 0.8])

    def body_xf(p):
        p = np.asarray(p, float)
        x = p[..., 0:1]
        w = np.clip(x / 0.5, -1, 1)          # -1 rear ... +1 front
        out = p.copy()
        if pose.flex:
            # arch: front drops nose-down, rear drops tail-down -> a bow up in the middle
            ang = -pose.flex * w[..., 0]
            for sgn in (1, -1):
                pass
            t = np.radians(ang)
            dx = out[..., 0] - pivot[0]
            dz = out[..., 2] - pivot[2]
            out[..., 0] = pivot[0] + np.cos(t) * dx - np.sin(t) * dz
            out[..., 2] = pivot[2] + np.sin(t) * dx + np.cos(t) * dz
        if pose.pitch:
            out = _rot_y(out, pose.pitch, np.array([0.0, 0.0, 0.6]))
        out[..., 2] += pose.body_z
        return out

    # --- torso
    L = loft(sp.torso, 28)
    x, zt, zb, hw = L.T
    zc = (zt + zb) / 2
    c = np.stack([x, np.zeros_like(x), zc], 1)
    c = body_xf(c)
    # frames: the torso's up is +z (after pitch it is close enough)
    torso = Tube("torso", c, (zt - zb) / 2, hw, np.array([0, 0, 1.0]), "torso")
    torso.xs = x  # remember the station x (for region materials)
    tubes.append(torso)

    # --- neck
    nb = body_xf(np.array([sp.neck_base[0], 0.0, sp.neck_base[1]]))
    ndir = _dir(pose.neck_ang + pose.pitch * 0.3, pose.neck_turn)
    # the neck is a slight S: base leaves the chest forward, then rises
    poll = nb + ndir * sp.neck_len
    mid = nb + ndir * sp.neck_len * 0.5 + np.array([0, 0, 0.02])
    npts = np.array([nb - ndir * 0.08, nb, mid, poll])
    na = np.array([sp.neck_r0[0] * 1.05, sp.neck_r0[0], (sp.neck_r0[0] + sp.neck_r1[0]) / 2, sp.neck_r1[0]])
    nbw = np.array([sp.neck_r0[1] * 1.05, sp.neck_r0[1], (sp.neck_r0[1] + sp.neck_r1[1]) / 2, sp.neck_r1[1]])
    # "up" normal of the neck: perpendicular to the neck in the sagittal plane, toward the mane side
    nup = np.cross(np.cross(ndir, [0, 0, 1.0]), ndir)
    if np.linalg.norm(nup) < 1e-6:
        nup = np.array([-1.0, 0, 0])
    nup /= np.linalg.norm(nup)
    neck = tube_from_points("neck", npts, na, nbw, nup, "neck", n=14)
    tubes.append(neck)
    if sp.mane > 0:
        # the bull's mane: a hanging dewlap of long hair under the neck, deepest mid-neck
        mpts = np.array([nb + ndir * 0.05, nb + ndir * sp.neck_len * 0.45, poll - ndir * 0.1]) - nup * np.array([[0.07], [0.09], [0.03]])
        tubes.append(tube_from_points("mane", mpts, [sp.mane * 1.4, sp.mane * 1.7, sp.mane * 0.7], [0.09, 0.09, 0.06], nup, DARK, n=10))

    # --- head
    hd = _dir(pose.head_ang + pose.pitch * 0.3, pose.neck_turn * 1.3)
    # head frame: "up" perpendicular to head axis, in the plane containing world up
    hup = np.cross(np.cross(hd, [0, 0, 1.0]), hd)
    if np.linalg.norm(hup) < 1e-6:
        hup = np.array([-1.0, 0, 0])
    hup /= np.linalg.norm(hup)
    h0 = poll + ndir * 0.02 + hup * 0.01
    hl = sp.head_len
    hpts = np.array([h0 - hd * 0.03, h0 + hd * hl * 0.25, h0 + hd * hl * 0.6, h0 + hd * hl * 0.9, h0 + hd * hl])
    ha = np.array([sp.head_r0[0] * 0.9, sp.head_r0[0], (sp.head_r0[0] * 0.4 + sp.head_r1[0] * 0.6), sp.head_r1[0], sp.head_r1[0] * 0.8])
    hb = np.array([sp.head_r0[1] * 0.9, sp.head_r0[1], (sp.head_r0[1] * 0.4 + sp.head_r1[1] * 0.6), sp.head_r1[1], sp.head_r1[1] * 0.85])
    # the face profile: brow over the eyes, a straight nose; muzzle hangs a little below the axis
    hpts[3:] -= hup * sp.head_r1[0] * 0.3
    head = tube_from_points("head", hpts, ha, hb, hup, "head", n=12)
    head.axis = hd
    tubes.append(head)
    if pose.mouth > 0:
        # bugling: lower jaw drops
        jd = _dir(pose.head_ang - 22 * pose.mouth, pose.neck_turn * 1.3)
        j0 = h0 + hd * hl * 0.35 - hup * 0.03
        jp = np.array([j0, j0 + jd * hl * 0.6])
        tubes.append(tube_from_points("jaw", jp, [sp.head_r1[0] * 0.7, sp.head_r1[0] * 0.45], [sp.head_r1[1] * 0.8, sp.head_r1[1] * 0.6], hup, "head", n=6))

    # --- ears: flat leaves from behind the eyes, up-back-out
    for side in (1, -1):
        base = h0 + hd * 0.02 + hup * sp.head_r0[0] * 0.75 + np.array([0, side * sp.head_r0[1] * 0.6, 0])
        back = -hd
        # direction: mix of back, up (hup) and out (side), modulated by pose
        ea = np.radians(pose.ear_ang)
        out = np.array([0, side, 0.0])
        d = np.cos(ea) * back * (1 - 0.8 * pose.ear_fwd) + np.sin(ea) * hup + 0.55 * out + pose.ear_fwd * (hd * 0.3 + hup * 0.6)
        if ind.species == "deer":
            d = d + 0.7 * out            # the blacktail's big ears stand out in a V
        d /= np.linalg.norm(d)
        el = sp.ear_len
        epts = np.array([base, base + d * el * 0.45, base + d * el])
        # flat: thin along the ear's normal, wide across; normal ~ perpendicular to d and hup
        enorm = np.cross(d, hup)
        enorm /= (np.linalg.norm(enorm) + 1e-9)
        tubes.append(tube_from_points(f"ear{side}", epts, [sp.ear_w * 0.12, sp.ear_w * 0.1, 0.004],
                                      [sp.ear_w * 0.8, sp.ear_w, sp.ear_w * 0.12], enorm, "ear", thin=True, n=7))

    # --- antlers
    ant = ind.antler()
    if ant:
        tubes += antlers(ind, sp, h0, hd, hup, ant)

    # --- tail
    tb = body_xf(np.array([sp.torso[-1][0] + 0.03, 0.0, sp.torso[-1][1] - 0.01]))
    tdir = _dir(-80 + 150 * pose.tail_up)
    if ind.species == "elk":
        tpts = np.array([tb - np.array([0.02, 0, 0]), tb + tdir * sp.tail_len])
        tubes.append(tube_from_points("tail", tpts, [sp.tail_w * 0.6, sp.tail_w * 0.4], [sp.tail_w * 0.7, sp.tail_w * 0.4], [1, 0, 0], "tail", n=5))
    else:
        tpts = np.array([tb - np.array([0.02, 0, 0]), tb + tdir * sp.tail_len * 0.5, tb + tdir * sp.tail_len])
        tubes.append(tube_from_points("tail", tpts, [0.018, 0.02, 0.014], [sp.tail_w * 0.7, sp.tail_w, sp.tail_w * 0.6],
                                      np.cross(tdir, [0, 1, 0]) if abs(tdir[1]) < 0.9 else [1, 0, 0], "tail", n=6))

    # --- legs
    for name in LEG_NAMES:
        tubes += leg(sp, pose, name, body_xf)

    # scale to metres
    for t in tubes:
        t.c = t.c * H
        t.a = t.a * H
        t.b = t.b * H
    return tubes, sp


def leg(sp, pose, name, body_xf):
    fore = name[1] == "F"
    side = 1 if name[0] == "L" else -1
    ax, az, ay = sp.fore_attach if fore else sp.hind_attach
    root = body_xf(np.array([ax, side * ay, az]))
    L1, L2, L3 = sp.fore_len if fore else sp.hind_len
    r = sp.fore_r if fore else sp.hind_r
    kind, *args = pose.legs[name]
    rx, rz = root[0], root[2]
    if kind == "ik":
        (fx, fz), past = args
        # hoof point on the ground: contact is the hoof's bottom
        ft = np.array([fx, max(fz, 0.0) + r[4] * 0.6])
        pa = np.radians(past)
        # pastern vector from fetlock to hoof: forward-down at +past
        pv = np.array([np.sin(pa), -np.cos(pa)]) * L3
        fet = ft - pv
        joint = ik2(np.array([rx, rz]), fet, L1, L2, bend_forward=fore)
        pts2 = [np.array([rx, rz]), joint, fet, ft]
    else:
        a1, a2, a3 = [np.radians(v) for v in args[0]]
        p0 = np.array([rx, rz])
        p1 = p0 + L1 * np.array([np.sin(a1), -np.cos(a1)])
        p2 = p1 + L2 * np.array([np.sin(a2), -np.cos(a2)])
        p3 = p2 + L3 * np.array([np.sin(a3), -np.cos(a3)])
        pts2 = [p0, p1, p2, p3]
        m = min(p[1] for p in pts2)
        if m < r[4] * 0.6:
            pass
    y = root[1]
    P = [np.array([p[0], y, p[1]]) for p in pts2]
    out = []
    up = np.array([0, 1.0, 0])  # legs: 'a' is lateral? no: use lateral as b; up normal = forward-ish
    # upper segment: forearm / gaskin, thick at the top, running up into the body
    top = P[0] + np.array([0, 0, 0.07 if fore else 0.1])
    seg1 = np.array([top, P[0], (P[0] + P[1]) / 2, P[1]])
    if fore:
        ra1 = [r[0] * 1.15, r[0], (r[0] + r[1]) / 2 * 0.95, r[1]]
    else:
        ra1 = [r[0] * 1.3, r[0] * 1.1, r[0] * 0.8, r[1]]
    out.append(tube_from_points(name + "1", seg1, ra1, np.array(ra1) * 0.8, [1, 0, 0], "leg1", n=10))
    # hock point: the calcaneus sticks out behind the hock
    if not fore:
        d = P[1] - P[0]
        d /= np.linalg.norm(d) + 1e-9
        back = np.array([-1.0, 0, 0])
        hp = P[1] + back * r[1] * 0.9 + d * r[1] * 0.2
        out.append(tube_from_points(name + "hk", np.array([P[1] - d * r[1] * 1.6, hp]), [r[1] * 0.7, r[1] * 0.45], [r[1] * 0.6, r[1] * 0.4], [0, 0, 1], "leg", thin=True, n=4))
    # cannon
    seg2 = np.array([P[1], P[2]])
    out.append(tube_from_points(name + "2", seg2, [r[1] * 0.95, r[2]], [r[1] * 0.85, r[2] * 0.9], [1, 0, 0], "leg", thin=True, n=8))
    # fetlock + pastern + hoof
    d3 = P[3] - P[2]
    seg3 = np.array([P[2], P[2] + d3 * 0.55, P[3]])
    out.append(tube_from_points(name + "3", seg3, [r[3], r[3] * 0.8, r[4]], [r[3] * 0.85, r[3] * 0.75, r[4] * 0.9], [1, 0, 0], "foot", thin=True, n=6))
    for t in out:
        t.leg = name
    return out


def antlers(ind, sp, h0, hd, hup, kind):
    """Elk: a main beam sweeping up and back past the shoulders with tines off its front;
    Roosevelts crown at the top. Deer: a small dichotomous fork."""
    out = []
    rng = np.random.default_rng(ind.seed * 7 + 3)
    back = -hd
    for side in (1, -1):
        out_v = np.array([0, side, 0.0])
        base = h0 + hd * 0.03 + hup * sp.head_r0[0] * 0.9 + out_v * sp.head_r0[1] * 0.35
        if ind.species == "elk":
            if kind == "spike":
                pts = np.array([base, base + hup * 0.18 + back * 0.08 + out_v * 0.04, base + hup * 0.34 + back * 0.12 + out_v * 0.05])
                out.append(tube_from_points(f"ant{side}", pts, [0.016, 0.012, 0.004], [0.016, 0.012, 0.004], hd, "antler", thin=True, n=6))
                continue
            scale = 1.0 if kind == "hard" else 0.85
            fat = 1.0 if kind == "hard" else 1.5
            # main beam: up and out, then sweeping back, then the tip curling forward-in
            wob = rng.normal(0, 0.02, 3)
            beam = [base,
                    base + scale * (hup * 0.20 + back * 0.10 + out_v * 0.10),
                    base + scale * (hup * 0.42 + back * 0.26 + out_v * 0.18) + wob[0],
                    base + scale * (hup * 0.62 + back * 0.42 + out_v * 0.20) + wob[1],
                    base + scale * (hup * 0.80 + back * 0.50 + out_v * 0.16) + wob[2],
                    base + scale * (hup * 0.92 + back * 0.46 + out_v * 0.10)]
            beam = np.array(beam)
            # the head is tilted; antlers are fixed to the skull so they follow hd/hup already
            out.append(tube_from_points(f"beam{side}", beam, np.array([0.030, 0.026, 0.022, 0.019, 0.016, 0.007]) * fat,
                                        np.array([0.030, 0.026, 0.022, 0.019, 0.016, 0.007]) * fat, hd, "antler", thin=True, n=14))
            # tines, off the front of the beam: brow, bez, trez (royal, longest), then the crown
            for k, (u, L, ang) in enumerate([(0.08, 0.26, 20), (0.2, 0.2, 30), (0.45, 0.30, 35), (0.68, 0.22, 45), (0.82, 0.16, 55)]):
                if k == 1 and rng.random() < 0.3:
                    continue
                i = u * (len(beam) - 1)
                i0 = int(i)
                f = i - i0
                p0 = beam[i0] * (1 - f) + beam[min(i0 + 1, len(beam) - 1)] * f
                a = np.radians(ang + rng.normal(0, 6))
                d = hd * np.cos(a) + hup * np.sin(a) + out_v * 0.15
                d /= np.linalg.norm(d)
                L = L * scale * (1 + rng.normal(0, 0.1))
                tp = np.array([p0, p0 + d * L * 0.6 + hup * 0.02, p0 + d * L + hup * 0.05])
                out.append(tube_from_points(f"tine{side}{k}", tp, np.array([0.014, 0.010, 0.004]) * fat, np.array([0.014, 0.010, 0.004]) * fat,
                                            hd, "antler", thin=True, n=6))
        else:
            if kind == "spike":
                pts = np.array([base, base + hup * 0.15 + back * 0.02 + out_v * 0.04])
                out.append(tube_from_points(f"ant{side}", pts, [0.012, 0.004], [0.012, 0.004], hd, "antler", thin=True, n=5))
                continue
            fat = 1.0 if kind == "hard" else 1.4
            # blacktail: beam up-back-out then forward; forks dichotomously
            b0 = base
            b1 = base + hup * 0.16 + back * 0.08 + out_v * 0.12
            b2 = b1 + hup * 0.12 + hd * 0.06 + out_v * 0.05
            out.append(tube_from_points(f"beam{side}", np.array([b0, b1, b2]), np.array([0.018, 0.014, 0.008]) * fat,
                                        np.array([0.018, 0.014, 0.008]) * fat, hd, "antler", thin=True, n=7))
            f1 = b1 + hd * 0.02
            f2 = f1 + hup * 0.10 + back * 0.05 + out_v * 0.02
            out.append(tube_from_points(f"fork{side}", np.array([f1, f2]), np.array([0.011, 0.005]) * fat, np.array([0.011, 0.005]) * fat,
                                        hd, "antler", thin=True, n=5))
            f3 = b2 + hd * 0.01
            f4 = f3 + hup * 0.07 + hd * 0.03
            out.append(tube_from_points(f"fork2{side}", np.array([f3, f4]), np.array([0.009, 0.004]) * fat, np.array([0.009, 0.004]) * fat,
                                        hd, "antler", thin=True, n=4))
            # brow tine: a small point low on the beam
            p0 = base + (b1 - base) * 0.25
            out.append(tube_from_points(f"brow{side}", np.array([p0, p0 + hup * 0.04 + hd * 0.03]), [0.007, 0.003], [0.007, 0.003],
                                        hd, "antler", thin=True, n=3))
    return out

Write: elements/deer-elk/src/render.py
"""Tubes -> supersampled surface splats -> pixel index canvas (material, step).

The pipeline:

  1. sample every tube's surface densely (points closer than half a supersample pixel),
     with normals and a hide material per point (coat.py);
  2. project orthographically for a camera yaw and elevation (0 = side view, 30 = iso);
     z-buffer the splats; a light-space z-buffer gives self- and ground-shadow;
  3. reduce each ss x ss block to one pixel: coverage, mean normal, majority material, depth;
  4. thin parts (lower legs, ears, antlers, tail) get their centrelines redrawn as clean runs
     so they never break up at small sizes;
  5. form classes (light 5, 4 | halftone 3 || reflected 2 | shadow 1 | core 0) from the lit
     normal, the shadow test, bounce from the ground and the sky.
"""
import numpy as np
from dataclasses import dataclass
import coat

STEPS = 6


@dataclass
class View:
    yaw: float = 0.0        # degrees; 0 = animal faces screen-right, side on
    elev: float = 8.0       # camera elevation in degrees (0 side, 30 iso)
    sun_az: float = 135.0   # sun azimuth in the world, degrees (0 = screen right, 90 = away from viewer)
    sun_el: float = 40.0
    ss: int = 6             # supersampling


def sun_vec(az, el):
    a, e = np.radians(az), np.radians(el)
    return np.array([np.cos(e) * np.cos(a), np.cos(e) * np.sin(a), np.sin(e)])


def _frames(c, up):
    t = np.gradient(c, axis=0)
    t /= np.linalg.norm(t, axis=1, keepdims=True) + 1e-12
    u = np.broadcast_to(up, c.shape).astype(float)
    n1 = u - (u * t).sum(1, keepdims=True) * t
    bad = np.linalg.norm(n1, axis=1) < 1e-4
    if bad.any():
        alt = np.array([0, 0, 1.0]) if abs(up[2]) < 0.9 else np.array([1.0, 0, 0])
        n1[bad] = alt - (alt * t[bad]).sum(1, keepdims=True) * t[bad]
    n1 /= np.linalg.norm(n1, axis=1, keepdims=True) + 1e-12
    n2 = np.cross(t, n1)
    return t, n1, n2


def sample_tube(tb, ds):
    """Points, normals, u (0..1 along), cos, sin on the tube surface at spacing ds (metres)."""
    c, a, b = tb.c, tb.a, tb.b
    seg = np.linalg.norm(np.diff(c, axis=0), axis=1)
    s = np.concatenate([[0], np.cumsum(seg)])
    Ltot = s[-1]
    n_al = max(int(np.ceil(Ltot / ds)) + 1, 2)
    t = np.linspace(0, Ltot, n_al)
    C = np.stack([np.interp(t, s, c[:, k]) for k in range(3)], 1)
    A = np.interp(t, s, a)
    B = np.interp(t, s, b)
    U = t / max(Ltot, 1e-9)
    T, N1, N2 = _frames(C, tb.up)
    per = 2 * np.pi * np.sqrt((A ** 2 + B ** 2) / 2)
    n_th = max(int(np.ceil(per.max() / ds)), 8)
    th = np.linspace(0, 2 * np.pi, n_th, endpoint=False)
    ct, st = np.cos(th), np.sin(th)
    P = C[:, None, :] + A[:, None, None] * ct[None, :, None] * N1[:, None, :] + B[:, None, None] * st[None, :, None] * N2[:, None, :]
    Nrm = ct[None, :, None] / (A[:, None, None] + 1e-9) * N1[:, None, :] + st[None, :, None] / (B[:, None, None] + 1e-9) * N2[:, None, :]
    # taper tilts the normal along the axis
    dA = np.gradient(A, t) if n_al > 1 else np.zeros_like(A)
    Nrm = Nrm - (dA[:, None, None] / (A[:, None, None] + 1e-9)) * T[:, None, :] * 1.0
    Nrm /= np.linalg.norm(Nrm, axis=2, keepdims=True) + 1e-12
    out_p = [P.reshape(-1, 3)]
    out_n = [Nrm.reshape(-1, 3)]
    out_u = [np.repeat(U, n_th)]
    out_c = [np.tile(ct, n_al)]
    out_s = [np.tile(st, n_al)]
    # caps: half-ellipsoids
    for end, sgn, flag in ((0, -1, tb.cap0), (-1, 1, tb.cap1)):
        if not flag:
            continue
        a0, b0 = A[end], B[end]
        r = min(a0, b0)
        m = max(int(np.ceil(r * np.pi / 2 / ds)), 2)
        for k in range(1, m + 1):
            ph = k / m * np.pi / 2
            cr, sr = np.cos(ph), np.sin(ph)
            nth = max(int(np.ceil(2 * np.pi * np.sqrt((a0 ** 2 + b0 ** 2) / 2) * cr / ds)), 4)
            thk = np.linspace(0, 2 * np.pi, nth, endpoint=False)
            p = C[end] + sgn * T[end] * r * sr + a0 * cr * np.cos(thk)[:, None] * N1[end] + b0 * cr * np.sin(thk)[:, None] * N2[end]
            nn = (np.cos(thk)[:, None] / (a0 + 1e-9) * N1[end] + np.sin(thk)[:, None] / (b0 + 1e-9) * N2[end]) * cr + sgn * T[end] * sr / (r + 1e-9)
            nn /= np.linalg.norm(nn, axis=1, keepdims=True) + 1e-12
            out_p.append(p)
            out_n.append(nn)
            out_u.append(np.full(nth, 0.0 if end == 0 else 1.0))
            out_c.append(np.cos(thk) * cr)
            out_s.append(np.sin(thk) * cr)
    return (np.concatenate(out_p), np.concatenate(out_n), np.concatenate(out_u), np.concatenate(out_c), np.concatenate(out_s))


def view_xf(view):
    """World (animal frame, metres) -> camera: returns function p -> (sx, sy_up, depth)."""
    y = np.radians(view.yaw)
    e = np.radians(view.elev)

    def f(p):
        # rotate the animal by yaw about z (0 = faces +x = screen right)
        x = p[..., 0] * np.cos(y) - p[..., 1] * np.sin(y)
        yy = p[..., 0] * np.sin(y) + p[..., 1] * np.cos(y)
        z = p[..., 2]
        # camera on the -y side looking +y, tilted down by e
        sx = x
        sv = z * np.cos(e) + yy * np.sin(e)
        d = yy * np.cos(e) - z * np.sin(e)
        return sx, sv, d
    return f


def rot_yaw(v, yaw):
    y = np.radians(yaw)
    x = v[..., 0] * np.cos(y) - v[..., 1] * np.sin(y)
    yy = v[..., 0] * np.sin(y) + v[..., 1] * np.cos(y)
    out = np.array(v, float, copy=True)
    out[..., 0] = x
    out[..., 1] = yy
    return out


@dataclass
class Raster:
    cov: np.ndarray       # (h,w) coverage 0..1
    mat: np.ndarray       # (h,w) int, -1 empty
    step: np.ndarray      # (h,w) int 0..5
    depth: np.ndarray
    lit: np.ndarray       # (h,w) float n.L (world)
    shadow: np.ndarray    # (h,w) fraction shadowed
    thin: np.ndarray      # (h,w) bool: pixel belongs to a thin part
    part: np.ndarray      # (h,w) int part index
    ground_shadow: np.ndarray  # (h,w) fraction of ground in cast shadow
    origin: tuple         # pixel of the world origin (x, y) for placement
    nrm: np.ndarray = None
    ground_row: int = 0   # the row of the ground contact (z = 0 under the body)
    parts: list = None


def rasterize(tubes, ind, view: View, k: float, pad=3, shadow_ground=True):
    """k: pixels per metre. Returns a Raster."""
    ss = view.ss
    kk = k * ss
    ds = 0.55 / kk
    P, N, M, TH, PART = [], [], [], [], []
    for i, tb in enumerate(tubes):
        p, n, u, cth, sth = sample_tube(tb, ds)
        m = coat.materials(ind, tb, p, n, u, cth, sth)
        P.append(p); N.append(n); M.append(m)
        TH.append(np.full(len(p), tb.thin)); PART.append(np.full(len(p), i))
    P = np.concatenate(P); N = np.concatenate(N); M = np.concatenate(M)
    TH = np.concatenate(TH); PART = np.concatenate(PART)
    f = view_xf(view)
    sx, sv, d = f(P)
    # screen extents (include the ground shadow area)
    L = sun_vec(view.sun_az, view.sun_el)
    Lw = rot_yaw(L[None], -view.yaw)[0]      # sun in the animal frame
    # ground points of the shadow: P projected along -L to z = 0
    if Lw[2] > 0.05:
        tpar = P[:, 2] / Lw[2]
        G = P - tpar[:, None] * Lw[None]
        G[:, 2] = 0
    else:
        G = P.copy(); G[:, 2] = 0
    gx, gv, gd = f(G)
    xmin = min(sx.min(), gx.min() if shadow_ground else sx.min())
    xmax = max(sx.max(), gx.max() if shadow_ground else sx.max())
    vmin = min(sv.min(), gv.min() if shadow_ground else sv.min())
    vmax = max(sv.max(), 0)
    W = int(np.ceil((xmax - xmin) * k)) + 2 * pad
    Hh = int(np.ceil((vmax - vmin) * k)) + 2 * pad
    ox = -xmin * k + pad        # pixel x of world x=0
    oy = vmax * k + pad         # pixel y of screen v=0 (image rows go down)
    # snap origin to the pixel grid so the ground line is exact
    ox = np.floor(ox) + 0.5 * 0
    oy = np.floor(oy)
    X = ((sx * k + ox) * ss).astype(np.int64)
    Y = ((oy - sv * k) * ss).astype(np.int64)
    Ws, Hs = W * ss, Hh * ss
    ok = (X >= 0) & (X < Ws) & (Y >= 0) & (Y < Hs)
    X, Y, dd = X[ok], Y[ok], d[ok]
    Pk, Nk, Mk, THk, PARTk = P[ok], N[ok], M[ok], TH[ok], PART[ok]
    # light-space shadow map: points seen from the sun
    shadowed = light_shadow(Pk, Lw, kk)
    order = np.argsort(-dd)          # far first; near overwrite
    idx = (Y * Ws + X)[order]
    zb = np.full(Ws * Hs, np.inf)
    mb = np.full(Ws * Hs, -1, np.int32)
    nb = np.zeros((Ws * Hs, 3))
    sb = np.zeros(Ws * Hs, np.float32)
    tb_ = np.zeros(Ws * Hs, bool)
    pb = np.full(Ws * Hs, -1, np.int32)
    zb[idx] = dd[order]
    mb[idx] = Mk[order]
    nb[idx] = rot_yaw(Nk[order], view.yaw) if False else Nk[order]
    sb[idx] = shadowed[order]
    tb_[idx] = THk[order]
    pb[idx] = PARTk[order]
    filled = mb >= 0
    # reduce blocks
    def blocks(a):
        return a.reshape(Hh, ss, W, ss, *a.shape[1:]).swapaxes(1, 2).reshape(Hh, W, ss * ss, *a.shape[1:])
    fb = blocks(filled)
    cov = fb.mean(2)
    zbk = blocks(np.where(filled, zb, np.inf))
    depth = zbk.min(2)
    nbk = blocks(nb)
    cnt = np.maximum(fb.sum(2), 1)
    nrm = (nbk * fb[..., None]).sum(2) / cnt[..., None]
    nrm /= np.linalg.norm(nrm, axis=2, keepdims=True) + 1e-9
    sh = (blocks(sb) * fb).sum(2) / cnt
    thn = (blocks(tb_) & fb).sum(2) / cnt > 0.5
    mbk = blocks(mb)
    pbk = blocks(pb)
    # majority material among filled samples, weighted toward the nearest third of depth
    mat = np.full((Hh, W), -1, np.int32)
    part = np.full((Hh, W), -1, np.int32)
    nm = coat.NMAT
    onehot = np.zeros((Hh, W, nm))
    for mm in range(nm):
        onehot[..., mm] = ((mbk == mm) & fb).sum(2)
    # a few materials are small but decisive (nose, eye, tips): boost them
    boost = np.ones(nm); boost[coat.PRIORITY] = 2.2
    mat_best = np.argmax(onehot * boost, 2)
    mat = np.where(cov > 0, mat_best, -1)
    # part: majority
    npart = len(tubes)
    pc = np.zeros((Hh, W, npart), np.int16)
    for pi in range(npart):
        pc[..., pi] = ((pbk == pi) & fb).sum(2)
    part = np.where(cov > 0, np.argmax(pc, 2), -1)
    lit = (nrm * Lw[None, None]).sum(2)
    # ground shadow raster
    gsh = np.zeros((Hh, W))
    if shadow_ground and Lw[2] > 0.05:
        GX = ((gx * k + ox) * ss).astype(np.int64)
        GY = ((oy - gv * k) * ss).astype(np.int64)
        okg = (GX >= 0) & (GX < Ws) & (GY >= 0) & (GY < Hs)
        gm = np.zeros(Ws * Hs, bool)
        gm[(GY[okg] * Ws + GX[okg])] = True
        # close small holes
        from scipy.ndimage import binary_closing
        gm = binary_closing(gm.reshape(Hs, Ws), iterations=2)
        gsh = gm.reshape(Hh, ss, W, ss).mean((1, 3))
    return Raster(cov=cov, mat=mat, step=np.zeros((Hh, W), np.int32), depth=depth, lit=lit, shadow=sh, thin=thn,
                  part=part, ground_shadow=gsh, origin=(ox, oy), nrm=nrm, ground_row=int(oy), parts=[t.name for t in tubes])


def light_shadow(P, Lw, kk):
    """Fraction-free shadow test: 1 where a point is occluded from the sun by other geometry."""
    Lw = Lw / np.linalg.norm(Lw)
    # light frame
    up = np.array([0, 0, 1.0]) if abs(Lw[2]) < 0.95 else np.array([1.0, 0, 0])
    e1 = np.cross(up, Lw); e1 /= np.linalg.norm(e1)
    e2 = np.cross(Lw, e1)
    a = P @ e1
    b = P @ e2
    dz = -(P @ Lw)                 # larger = farther from the sun
    res = kk * 0.7
    ia = ((a - a.min()) * res).astype(np.int64)
    ib = ((b - b.min()) * res).astype(np.int64)
    Wl = ia.max() + 1
    key = ib * Wl + ia
    zmin = np.full(key.max() + 1, np.inf)
    np.minimum.at(zmin, key, dz)
    bias = 3.0 / res
    return (dz > zmin[key] + bias).astype(np.float32)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------- lines for thin parts

def clean_line(x0, y0, x1, y1):
    """Pixels of a line snapped to clean runs (Bresenham on integer endpoints)."""
    x0, y0, x1, y1 = int(round(x0)), int(round(y0)), int(round(x1)), int(round(y1))
    pts = []
    dx, dy = abs(x1 - x0), -abs(y1 - y0)
    sx = 1 if x0 < x1 else -1
    sy = 1 if y0 < y1 else -1
    err = dx + dy
    while True:
        pts.append((x0, y0))
        if x0 == x1 and y0 == y1:
            break
        e2 = 2 * err
        if e2 >= dy:
            err += dy; x0 += sx
        if e2 <= dx:
            err += dx; y0 += sy
    return pts


def thin_lines(R, tubes, ind, view, k):
    """Redraw thin parts narrower than ~1.6 px as 1-px clean runs through their centrelines.
    Occlusion: only where the line is nearer than what is already there."""
    f = view_xf(view)
    ox, oy = R.origin
    H, W = R.cov.shape
    L = sun_vec(view.sun_az, view.sun_el)
    Lw = rot_yaw(L[None], -view.yaw)[0]
    for i, tb in enumerate(tubes):
        if not tb.thin:
            continue
        wpx = 2 * np.maximum(tb.a, tb.b).mean() * k
        if wpx > 1.8:
            continue
        sx, sv, d = f(tb.c)
        px = sx * k + ox
        py = oy - sv * k
        # the material along the line: sample the tube's own materials on its axis-facing side
        u = np.linspace(0, 1, len(tb.c))
        mats = coat.axis_materials(ind, tb, u)
        tang = np.gradient(tb.c, axis=0)
        # lighting of a thin cylinder: max over the ring, approximated by (1 - (t.L)^2)^0.5
        tn = tang / (np.linalg.norm(tang, axis=1, keepdims=True) + 1e-9)
        tl = (tn @ Lw)
        litv = np.sqrt(np.clip(1 - tl ** 2, 0, 1)) * 0.8 - 0.1
        # stop the line where the tube thins below ~0.35 px (tips) unless it is an antler tip
        for j in range(len(tb.c) - 1):
            if 2 * max(tb.a[j], tb.b[j]) * k < 0.25 and tb.name[:3] not in ("tin", "bea", "for", "bro", "ant"):
                continue
            for (x, y) in clean_line(px[j], py[j], px[j + 1], py[j + 1]):
                if 0 <= x < W and 0 <= y < H:
                    dj = d[j]
                    if R.cov[y, x] < 0.45 or dj < R.depth[y, x] - 0.02:
                        R.mat[y, x] = mats[j]
                        R.cov[y, x] = max(R.cov[y, x], 0.9)
                        R.depth[y, x] = min(R.depth[y, x], dj)
                        R.thin[y, x] = True
                        R.part[y, x] = i
                        R.lit[y, x] = litv[j]
                        R.shadow[y, x] = 0.0
    return R
Write: elements/deer-elk/src/coat.py
"""Hide regions: which material each surface point wears.

Roosevelt elk: warm tan body; dark chocolate head, neck, mane, brisket, belly and legs; a pale
straw rump patch edged dark; a small tail the colour of the patch. Cows the same pattern, less
contrast. Calves: red-brown with cream spots in rows along the back.

Black-tailed deer: grey-brown (winter) or rusty (summer) all over, paler belly and inner legs, a
white throat patch, a dark forehead on bucks, a black nose, big ears with pale insides and dark
rims, a broad tail black on top and white beneath (the white shows when it is flagged).
"""
import numpy as np
from rig import (BODY, DARK, PALE, BELLY, LEG, HOOF, ANTLER, TIP, NOSE, EARIN, THROAT, TAILTOP,
                 SPOT, VELVET, EYE)

NMAT = 15
PRIORITY = [NOSE, EYE, TIP, TAILTOP, THROAT]


def _hash(a, b, seed):
    h = (np.floor(a).astype(np.int64) * 73856093) ^ (np.floor(b).astype(np.int64) * 19349663) ^ (seed * 83492791)
    return ((h % 1000003) / 1000003.0 + 1) % 1.0


def materials(ind, tb, p, n, u, cth, sth):
    sp = ind.spec()
    H = sp.H
    q = p / H
    nm = tb.name
    elk = ind.species == "elk"
    young = ind.age == "young"
    out = np.full(len(p), BODY, np.int32)
    if nm == "torso":
        x = q[:, 0]
        z = q[:, 2]
        # height within the local section: cth is along the up normal (+1 top, -1 bottom)
        up = cth
        xr = sp.torso[-1][0]      # rear end x
        xf = sp.torso[0][0]
        if elk:
            out[:] = BODY
            out[(x > xf - 0.14) & (up < 0.6)] = DARK                        # brisket
            out[(x > xf - 0.22) & (up < -0.2)] = DARK
            out[(up < -0.62) & (x > xr + 0.2)] = BELLY                      # dark belly line
            # rump patch: the rear, above the thighs; its edge dark
            rp = (x < xr + 0.13) & (up > -0.45)
            edge = (x < xr + 0.19) & (x >= xr + 0.13) & (up > -0.2) & (np.abs(sth) > 0.45)
            out[edge] = DARK
            out[rp] = PALE
            out[(x < xr + 0.2) & (up <= -0.45)] = DARK                      # back of the thighs
            if young:
                h = _hash(x * 22, (np.arctan2(sth, cth)) * 5.5, ind.seed)
                spots = (up > 0.15) & (up < 0.9) & (x > xr + 0.15) & (x < xf - 0.12) & (h < 0.33)
                out[spots] = SPOT
        else:
            out[:] = BODY
            out[(up < -0.55)] = BELLY
            out[(x < xr + 0.1) & (up < 0.1) & (np.abs(sth) < 0.5)] = PALE   # inner thighs / under tail
            out[(x > xf - 0.1) & (up < -0.1)] = BODY
            if young:
                h = _hash(x * 26, (np.arctan2(sth, cth)) * 6.0, ind.seed)
                spots = (up > 0.1) & (up < 0.92) & (x > xr + 0.08) & (x < xf - 0.1) & (h < 0.36)
                out[spots] = SPOT
    elif nm in ("neck", "mane"):
        if elk:
            out[:] = DARK
            if young:
                out[:] = BODY
        else:
            out[:] = BODY
            # the white throat patch: high on the throat, under the jaw
            out[(u > 0.72) & (cth < -0.35)] = THROAT
    elif nm in ("head", "jaw"):
        out[:] = DARK if elk else BODY
        if elk and young:
            out[:] = BODY
        # nose: the last 8% of the head, lower half; eye: a point on the side, a third of the way
        out[(u > 0.93)] = NOSE
        eye = (np.abs(u - 0.3) < 0.07) & (cth > 0.05) & (cth < 0.55) & (np.abs(sth) > 0.8)
        out[eye] = EYE
        if not elk:
            # dark forehead (bucks; a faint one on does), pale muzzle band behind the nose
            out[(u > 0.1) & (u < 0.5) & (cth > 0.75) & (ind.sex == "m")] = DARK
            out[(u > 0.82) & (u <= 0.93)] = THROAT
        else:
            out[(u > 0.82) & (u <= 0.93) & (cth < 0.1)] = PALE if not young else BODY
    elif nm.startswith("ear"):
        # inner face: normal toward the head's forward axis / up; rims dark on deer
        out[:] = DARK if (elk and not young) else BODY
        inner = cth > 0
        out[inner & (u < 0.85) & (np.abs(sth) < 0.8)] = EARIN
        if not elk:
            out[(u > 0.9) | (np.abs(sth) > 0.92)] = DARK
    elif nm == "tail":
        if elk:
            out[:] = PALE if not young else BODY
        else:
            # black top (the dorsal side: cth > 0 means along the tube's up = away from the rump)
            out[:] = TAILTOP
            out[cth < -0.1] = PALE
    elif nm[:2] in ("LF", "RF", "LH", "RH"):
        seg = nm[2:]
        if elk:
            out[:] = LEG
            if seg == "1":
                out[u < 0.35] = DARK if young is False else BODY
                if young:
                    out[:] = BODY
        else:
            out[:] = LEG
            if seg == "1":
                out[:] = BODY
                out[(u > 0.45) & (np.abs(sth) < 0.5)] = PALE if False else BODY
        if seg == "3":
            out[u > 0.72] = HOOF
    elif nm[:4] in ("beam", "tine", "fork", "brow") or nm.startswith("ant"):
        state = ind.antler()
        if state == "velvet":
            out[:] = VELVET
        else:
            out[:] = ANTLER
            if nm[:4] in ("tine", "fork", "brow") or nm.startswith("ant"):
                out[u > 0.72] = TIP
            if nm.startswith("beam"):
                out[u > 0.9] = TIP
    return out


def axis_materials(ind, tb, u):
    """The dominant material along a thin tube's axis (for 1-px lines)."""
    n = len(u)
    fake_p = tb.c
    cth = np.zeros(n) + 0.6
    sth = np.ones(n)
    return materials(ind, tb, fake_p, np.zeros((n, 3)), u, cth, sth)
Write: elements/deer-elk/src/paint.py
"""The painter: an Individual + gait/pose + view + size -> index canvas -> RGB.

index canvas: mat (int, -1 = empty) and step (0..5). The palette turns (mat, step) into colour.
"""
import numpy as np
from dataclasses import dataclass, replace
import rig, render, coat
from render import View

GROUND = 20          # material ids beyond the hide: ground and its cast shadow
GSHADOW = 21
CONTACT = 22


def classes(R, view):
    """Form classes: light 5, 4 | halftone 3 || reflected 2 | shadow 1 | core 0."""
    v = R.lit
    n = R.nrm
    sh = R.shadow > 0.5
    step = np.zeros(v.shape, np.int32)
    litf = (v > 0.03) & ~sh
    step[litf & (v > 0.74)] = 5
    step[litf & (v <= 0.74) & (v > 0.36)] = 4
    step[litf & (v <= 0.36)] = 3
    shf = ~litf
    step[shf] = 1
    # core: just past the terminator on unshadowed form
    step[shf & ~sh & (v > -0.28)] = 0
    # reflected light: surfaces facing down take bounce from the lit ground; the far side takes sky
    bounce = n[..., 2] < -0.4
    step[shf & bounce] = 2
    return step


def paint_index(ind, gait="stand", phase=0.0, view=View(), size_px=60, pose=None, lines=True):
    sp = ind.spec()
    if pose is None:
        pose = rig.gait_pose(sp, gait, phase)
    tubes, sp = rig.build(ind, pose)
    # size_px: the adult elk-cow body length convention -> px per metre for THIS individual:
    # we size by the individual's own nose-to-rump length in a side view (so a 4-px deer is 4 px).
    k = size_px / (sp.H * 1.62)
    R = render.rasterize(tubes, ind, view, k)
    if lines:
        R = render.thin_lines(R, tubes, ind, view, k)
    on = (R.cov >= 0.45) | R.thin
    R.mat = np.where(on, R.mat, -1)
    R.step = classes(R, view)
    return R, tubes, k


GREY_BASE = {  # luminance of step 4 per material, 0..1 (values from the photos' hide, in grey)
    rig.BODY: 0.56, rig.DARK: 0.26, rig.PALE: 0.80, rig.BELLY: 0.30, rig.LEG: 0.24, rig.HOOF: 0.10,
    rig.ANTLER: 0.36, rig.TIP: 0.86, rig.NOSE: 0.08, rig.EARIN: 0.62, rig.THROAT: 0.86, rig.TAILTOP: 0.10,
    rig.SPOT: 0.84, rig.VELVET: 0.40, rig.EYE: 0.05,
}
STEP_F = np.array([0.46, 0.56, 0.68, 0.80, 1.0, 1.16])


def grey(R, bg=0.62, flat=False):
    H, W = R.mat.shape
    img = np.full((H, W), bg, float)
    # ground shadow
    img = np.where(R.ground_shadow > 0.4, bg * 0.62, img)
    on = R.mat >= 0
    base = np.vectorize(lambda m: GREY_BASE.get(m, 0.5))(np.where(on, R.mat, 0))
    f = STEP_F[R.step] if not flat else 1.0
    img = np.where(on, np.clip(base * f, 0, 1), img)
    return (img * 255).astype(np.uint8)


def notan(R, bg=230):
    img = np.full(R.mat.shape, bg, np.uint8)
    img[R.mat >= 0] = 30
    return img

"""Side by side: Muybridge 692a frame 15 | my elk bull trot at the same shoulder height."""
import numpy as np, sys
from PIL import Image
import rig, paint
from render import View
ph=Image.open('../refs/elk_trotting/openverse_Elk_trotting_rbm_QP301M8_1887_692a_15_2699e75e.jpg').convert('L')
# photo: ground ~745, withers ~370 -> 375 px shoulder height; scale to 200
s=200/375
ph=ph.resize((int(ph.width*s),int(ph.height*s)))
phase=float(sys.argv[1]) if len(sys.argv)>1 else 0.3
ind=rig.Individual('elk','m','adult','autumn',1)
sp=ind.spec()
size=200*1.62  # size_px such that H maps to 200 px
R,_,k=paint.paint_index(ind,'trot',phase,View(yaw=0,elev=0,sun_el=60,sun_az=-100,ss=3),size_px=size)
g=paint.grey(R,bg=0.9)
H=max(ph.height,g.shape[0])
out=np.full((H, ph.width+g.shape[1]+10),255,np.uint8)
out[H-ph.height:,:ph.width]=np.array(ph)
# align ground rows: photo ground at 745*s
gr=int(745*s); 
off=gr-R.ground_row
y0=max(0,H-ph.height+off)
gg=g[:H-y0]
out[y0:y0+gg.shape[0], ph.width+10:ph.width+10+gg.shape[1]]=gg
Image.fromarray(out).save('../stages/s1/cmp_muybridge.png')
EOF

Bash: Shorten torso, fix sampling gaps
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/deer-elk/src && python3 - <<'EOF'
s=open('rig.py').read()
# scale torso x by 0.86 and the x of attachments
import re
def scale_block(name):
    global s
    i=s.index(name+' = ['); j=s.index(']',i)
    blk=s[i:j]
    def f(m):
        return '(%.3f,'%(float(m.group(1))*0.86)
    blk2=re.sub(r'\((-?\d+\.\d+),',f,blk)
    s=s[:i]+blk2+s[j:]
scale_block('ELK_TORSO'); scale_block('DEER_TORSO')
s=s.replace('neck_base=(0.42, 0.79)','neck_base=(0.36, 0.80)').replace('neck_base=(0.40, 0.79)','neck_base=(0.35, 0.80)')
s=s.replace('fore_attach=(0.36, 0.60, 0.105), hind_attach=(-0.50, 0.70, 0.11)','fore_attach=(0.30, 0.58, 0.105), hind_attach=(-0.43, 0.70, 0.11)')
s=s.replace('fore_attach=(0.33, 0.60, 0.09), hind_attach=(-0.46, 0.71, 0.095)','fore_attach=(0.28, 0.58, 0.09), hind_attach=(-0.40, 0.71, 0.095)')
s=s.replace('head_len=0.40, head_r0=(0.085, 0.075), head_r1=(0.05, 0.042)','head_len=0.38, head_r0=(0.09, 0.075), head_r1=(0.058, 0.044)')
open('rig.py','w').write(s)
r=open('render.py').read()
r=r.replace("    n_al = max(int(np.ceil(Ltot / ds)) + 1, 2)","    prof = np.abs(np.diff(a)).sum() + np.abs(np.diff(b)).sum()\n    n_al = max(int(np.ceil((Ltot + 2 * prof) / ds)) + 1, 2)")
open('render.py','w').write(r)

cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/deer-elk/src && cat > board.py <<'EOF'
"""Reference board: photo | mine, at the same shoulder height and ground line (greyscale form check)."""
import numpy as np, sys
from PIL import Image
import rig, paint
from render import View
REF = '~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/deer-elk/refs/'
NAT = '~/work/naturalist/animals/photos/'
BOARD = [
    # file, crop, ground row, withers row, individual, gait, phase, yaw
    (REF + 'roosevelt_elk_cow/openverse_Roosevelt_elk_cow_calf_5b89d184.jpg', (220, 110, 730, 560), 545, 228, ('elk', 'f'), 'alert', 0, 0),
    (REF + 'roosevelt_elk_cow/openverse_Majestic_Bull_21c2381d.jpg', (110, 20, 690, 630), 612, 282, ('elk', 'm'), 'stand', 0, 0),
    (REF + 'elk_trotting/openverse_Elk_trotting_rbm_QP301M8_1887_692a_15_2699e75e.jpg', (0, 20, 918, 780), 745, 372, ('elk', 'm'), 'trot', 0.3, 0),
    (NAT + 'blacktail_deer/commons_Columbian_black_tailed_deer_Odocoileus_hemionus__e3d0e22e.jpg', (190, 150, 800, 570), 555, 300, ('deer', 'm'), 'walk', 0.1, 180),
]
HPX = 150


def panel(i, phase=None, gait=None):
    f, crop, gr, wr, (spn, sex), g, ph, yaw = BOARD[i]
    im = Image.open(f).convert('L').crop(crop)
    s = HPX / (gr - wr)
    im = im.resize((int(im.width * s), int(im.height * s)))
    grow = int((gr - crop[1]) * s)
    ind = rig.Individual(spn, sex, 'adult', 'autumn', 1)
    R, _, k = paint.paint_index(ind, gait or g, ph if phase is None else phase, View(yaw=yaw, elev=0, sun_el=50, sun_az=-140, ss=3), size_px=HPX * 1.62)
    gimg = paint.grey(R, bg=0.92)
    H = max(im.height, gimg.shape[0] + max(0, grow - R.ground_row)) + 4
    out = np.full((H, im.width + gimg.shape[1] + 12), 255, np.uint8)
    out[:im.height, :im.width] = np.array(im)
    y0 = grow - R.ground_row
    ys, ye = max(0, y0), min(H, y0 + gimg.shape[0])
    out[ys:ye, im.width + 12:im.width + 12 + gimg.shape[1]] = gimg[ys - y0:ye - y0]
    return out


if __name__ == '__main__':
    tag = sys.argv[1] if len(sys.argv) > 1 else 'b'
    ps = [panel(i) for i in range(len(BOARD))]
    W = max(p.shape[1] for p in ps)
    out = np.full((sum(p.shape[0] for p in ps), W), 255, np.uint8)
    y = 0
    for p in ps:
        out[y:y + p.shape[0], :p.shape[1]] = p
        y += p.shape[0]
    Image.fromarray(out).save(f'../stages/s1/board_{tag}.png')
EOF
nice -n 10 env OPENBLAS_NUM_THREADS=1 python3 board.py b1

    C = np.stack([np.interp(t, s, c[:, k]) for k in range(3)], 1)

    if len(s) >= 3 and np.all(np.diff(s) > 1e-9):
        from scipy.interpolate import PchipInterpolator

        A = PchipInterpolator(s, a)(t)
        B = PchipInterpolator(s, b)(t)
    else:
        C = np.stack([np.interp(t, s, c[:, k]) for k in range(3)], 1)
        A = np.interp(t, s, a)
        B = np.interp(t, s, b)''')
