# Roosevelt elk and black-tailed deer, after Mark Ferrari: lessons

A student's log. Subject: Roosevelt elk (bull, cow, yearling, calf) and Columbian black-tailed
deer (buck, doe, fawn), as individuals and as herds, from a 60 px hero animal down to a 4 px dot,
in the landscape view (camera 6-10 deg) and the 30 deg iso view.

Everything here is made by the programs in `src/`. Stage images are in `stages/`, parameter
sheets in `sheets/`, photos in `refs/` (fetched with the studio's imgfetch; licences in each
`sources.tsv`) plus the naturalist's `~/work/naturalist/animals/photos/`.



cd src
nice -n 10 python3 sheets.py            # all sheets A-H (~4 min, 4 processes)
nice -n 10 python3 sheets.py G          # just the herds
python3 s0_copy.py                      # the stage-0 master copy




The technique as code (one call paints one animal; one more paints a herd):



import rig, paint, herd
from render import View
ind = rig.Individual(&quot;elk&quot;, &quot;m&quot;, &quot;adult&quot;, season=&quot;autumn&quot;, seed=2)     # species, sex, age, season, seed
R, tubes, k = paint.paint_index(ind, gait=&quot;trot&quot;, phase=0.3,
                                view=View(yaw=-14, elev=8, sun_az=-150, sun_el=30, diffuse=0),
                                size_px=48)                             # body length in px
rgb = paint.colour(R, ind, hour=&quot;golden&quot;, ground=&quot;autumn&quot;, depth=0.0)   # palette is the light
img = herd.scene(herd.elk_herd(&quot;graze&quot;, 26, seed=7), k=6, W=320, H=180, elev=30, hour=&quot;morning&quot;)




| module | what it is |
|---|---|
| `rig.py` | the animal: species proportions (torso lofted from photo stations), sex/age/season/seed variation, poses and gaits (IK legs), antlers |
| `render.py` | tubes -> supersampled surface splats -> z-buffer, light-space shadow, pixel reduction; thin parts redrawn as clean runs |
| `coat.py` | hide regions in pose-free body coordinates: the elk's cape, rump patch, dark belly; deer throat, tail, ears; fawn spots |
| `hide.py` | pixel rules from the Ferrari copy: plane flattening, edge bands, lit edges, back-light rim, hair-dash dither |
| `fringe.py` | fur edges: interlocking teeth between hide colours, the bull's hanging mane |
| `micro.py` | the glyph painter under 14 px: body mass + skeleton strokes |
| `palette.py` | (material, step) -> colour per coat, hour, season and depth |
| `herd.py` | herd arrangements from the naturalist's guide, and the compositor |
| `ground.py` | a calm meadow to stand on (not my subject: grass-lod owns grass) |
| `sheets.py` | the labelled sheets |

---

## Stage 0: a master copy, because Ferrari never painted an elk

There is no animal in any Ferrari scene in the studio (`pedagogy/master-copy/ref/lw`, `shared/refs`).
The closest passage to "a warm-coloured, rounded, sunlit mass the size of a 60-px elk's body"
is the orange boulder in front of the stone gate in *V19* (native (238,255)-(280,290)), so I
copied that from its index map (`src/s0_copy.py`, loader borrowed from the willow student).

![boulder copy](stages/s0/s0_boulder_copy_8x.png)

*left: Ferrari; right: my copy from his silhouette and palette.*

What reading his indices showed:

- **The lit face is one gradient, not a dome.** Almost the whole face is a mix of two adjacent
  steps (`c` and `f`), pure `f` only on the brightest margin and pure `c` where it turns. The mix
  is a 2x2 Bayer (DPaint) fill: rows alternate `cccc` / `fcfc` at 25 %, a checker at 50 %.
  Fitting the f-density as a *linear* function of position gives 3.6 % per px to the right and
  reproduces 81 % of his face pixels exactly. A dome model got 50 %.
- **The form turns only at the edges**, in 1-2 px bands: a dull rose `a` one step down, then
  `8`, a **saturated** red-brown on the outermost shadow-side pixels. The reflected colour is more
  saturated than the lit ones. My palettes now boost chroma on step 2 by 30 %.
- **Lost and found edges**: against the lighter snow the lit face simply stops (no outline);
  against the dark the shadow side has its saturated rim.

Overall match 68 % of his pixels (face 81 %). The misses are all at the edge bands and in the
chips on the top-left. This stage gave me `hide.py`: Bayer gradients on lit planes, the
flattened interior, the shadow-edge band, the saturated reflected step. Not everything
transferred: see stage 3 (hair is not rock).

---

## Stage 1: the silhouette, in notan and greyscale, against photos

The naturalist's first principle: "behaviour is legible in silhouette, and at a distance it is
all you have": the ratio of body to leg to neck, the head height against the back line, the one
high-contrast patch, and how the animal sits with the others.

### How the form is built (`rig.py`)

- **Not capsules.** The torso is lofted from 13 stations (x, back height, belly height,
  half-width) read off side-view photos: the level back with the withers a touch above the croup,
  the deep chest ahead of the forelegs (the brisket), the belly rising into the flank tuck, the ham
  dropping behind it, the croup sloping to the tail root, the rounded buttock.
- **The neck is a wedge**: very deep where it leaves the withers and the brisket, tapering fast,
  then nearly parallel to the throat latch. Its top line starts at the withers, so there is no
  notch between back and neck. The bull's mane is a separate hanging tube under it.
- **Legs are anatomy**: a forearm deep fore-and-aft at the top (muscle), a carpus knob, a slim
  flat-sided cannon, a fetlock knob, a pastern and a small dark wedge of a hoof. The hind leg
  starts as the ham (its rear edge continuous with the buttock), tapers to the hock, which has its
  point (the calcaneus) sticking out behind, then a long cannon. IK places each hoof; forelegs bend
  forward at the carpus, hind legs back at the hock.
- **Antlers** follow the skull: the beam sweeps up and back and spreads wide; each tine leaves the
  beam turned forward by an angle measured from the beam itself (brow tine ~95 deg, the upper ones
  ~50) and curls back toward it. Roosevelt crowns at the top. Deer: a small dichotomous fork.

### The comparisons that shaped it

![notan, early](stages/s1/board_notan1.png) ![notan, later](stages/s1/board_notan5.png)

*Photo | mine, same shoulder height and ground line: cow (alert), bull (standing), Muybridge's elk
trotting (plate 692, frame 15), blacktail buck walking. Left: early; right: after the fixes below.*

What the photos corrected, in order (each one was visible in the notan and invisible in my head):

1. **The torso was 1.4 shoulder heights long.** Photos measure 1.05-1.3 H (the Muybridge bull
   ~1.3, a Roosevelt cow 1.05). A long torso on straight legs is what made the capsule rig read as
   a horse. Now ~1.1 H for elk, ~1.0 H for deer.
2. **The neck left the chest from the wrong place.** It must rise from the withers on top and the
   brisket below, as one wedge; a tube stuck on the front of a box reads llama.
3. **The rump was a box end.** The croup slopes and the buttock rounds down into the hams; the
   ham's rear edge runs down to the hock. The knob I had at the rear was the torso's cap sticking
   out above a ham placed too far forward.
4. **Legs were 50 % too thick.** An elk's cannon is ~0.035 H across. At 60 px that is a 1-px
   line, and a 2-px dark leg is the heaviest mark on the animal.
5. **The hind leg was a post.** The gaskin must be longer than the stifle's height allows, so the
   leg bends: the hock sits behind the line from stifle to hoof.
6. **Antlers stood up like a hat-rack**, then pointed their tines straight forward like a comb.
   Tines leave the beam at an angle measured from the beam, not from the head.

Still not right at stage 1: the bull's neck and mane are thinner than the photos' (a Roosevelt
bull's neck in autumn is almost as deep as his chest); the deer's head is a touch long.

![grey board](stages/s1/board_grey5.png)

---

## Stage 2: colour, and the far read

![colour at sizes](stages/s1/sizesc_c1.png)

*Photo reduced by area-averaging | mine at 60, 30, 16, 8 px (4x).*

- **Hide colours read off the photos** (`palette.py`): the cow pale greyish tan with a mid
  chocolate neck; the autumn bull orange-tan, near cream in sun, with a black-brown mane; the pale
  straw rump patch edged dark; dark legs and belly. Blacktails grey-brown in autumn/winter,
  startling rust in summer.
- **Regions are anatomical, not boxes** (`coat.py`, in pose-free body coordinates): the elk's cape
  runs back over the shoulder on a diagonal from just behind the withers to behind the elbow; the
  rump patch is an oval about the tail, dark-bordered on the thighs and open at the croup. My first
  regions were vertical cuts at fixed x and looked pasted on.
- **The far read survives.** At 16 and 8 px the reduced photo is a pale body with a darker front
  end; so is mine, which is more legible than the photo, as pixel art should be.

The first colour renders were **flat**: the sun from the front-left lit the whole visible side
the same step. Swinging the sun to about 25 deg in front of side-on (azimuth -150 to -160)
gives a lit back, a halftone side and a shaded belly: the light across the form that the photos
have. This is now the default for side views.

---

## Stage 3: the hide at hero size (60-30 px)

![hero, flat](stages/s1/zoom_z2.png)
![hero, lit + hide rules](stages/s1/zoom_z7.png)

- **Bayer planes and edge bands** from stage 0 (`hide.py`). On a rock the interior is flattened
  hard toward one value (the facet). **An elk's barrel is not a facet**: with the same flattening
  the side went dead. Flattening is 0.5 on the copy and 0.2 on hide.
- **Hair is not rock.** The 2x2 checker on a hide reads as sandstone. `hide.hair_quant` keeps the
  order but makes the cells 1 px wide and 2 px tall, staggered per column: the mixed tone becomes
  short vertical dashes, the hair lying down the flank.
- **Fur edges** (`fringe.py`): where cape meets body, rump meets thigh, body meets belly, the
  boundary interlocks in 1-px teeth on alternate 2-row blocks; the bull's mane hangs below the
  neck line as irregular 1-3 px dark ticks.
- **Found edges for dark hide**: a dark neck or leg against grass has almost the same value as the
  grass. The sun-side silhouette pixel of dark hide steps up to at least the halftone, so the neck
  separates without an outline.
- **Far-side limbs, ear and antler sit one step down.**
- **Antlers are drawn, not rasterized**: 1-px clean runs through the tine centrelines, 2 px near
  the base (the second pixel a step darker), beams dark, only the tips ivory. Rasterized, they were
  a blur with white blobs at the ends.
- **Contact**: an occlusion shadow under the torso whatever the sun (the animal must sit on the
  meadow), plus the true cast shadow from a light-space z-buffer.
- **Back light** (`hide.apply`): with the sun behind the animal every silhouette edge on the sun's
  side and over the top glows at the top step, with a halftone pixel inside it at >= 36 px. This
  is Ferrari's V02 rim, and at golden hour it is the whole picture:

![golden rim](stages/s1/golden_rim2.png)

### Honest comparison at 60 px

![cow](stages/s1/trip_t2_0.png)
![bull](stages/s1/trip_t2_1.png)
![buck](stages/s1/trip_t2_3.png)

*photo reduced to 60 px | mine | Ferrari's boulder, for craft (6x).*

**Not there yet.** Against the photo the bull is lighter in the body and less weighty: his
shoulder and ham are lit masses with darker ribs between, mine is one sausage of close values.
Against Ferrari my light and shadow families are too close and my planes too few; his boulder
has three clear planes and an edge band, my barrel has a gradient. The deer's summer rust reads
well; its face and ears are still weaker than the photo's.

---

## Stage 4: every size, 60 px to 4 px

![blobs](stages/s1/ladder_micro1.png)

*The splat renderer at 16..4 px: legs and antlers turn into blobs below ~12 px.*

A pixel artist at 8 px does not render an elk, he places it. `micro.py` is a glyph painter fed by
the same rig's keypoints:

- **body**: per column, the torso's projected top and bottom, rounded, biased half a pixel heavier
  at the belly (small figures with photo-true bodies read as spiders: the legs round long);
  corners knocked off at >= 9 px;
- **zones**: elk: the rear ~18 % is the pale rump, the front ~28 % the dark cape; top row in sun;
- **legs**: per pair, the near leg as a clean 1-px run from belly to hoof; the far leg only when
  its hoof is 2+ px from the near one (walking, running), one step darker; the hock only >= 12 px
  (below that it reads as a tail curling up);
- **neck, head**: 1-px runs; the head gets a jaw pixel at >= 9 px and the neck a second run at its
  base at >= 10 px (a mass, not a stroke);
- **antlers**: the beam as a 1-px run at >= 8 px, a single dark pixel above the head below that;
- **ears**: a 1-px tick for deer at >= 8 px; a flagged tail is a pale run.

![ladder](stages/s1/ladder_l2.png)

*The ladder: 60, 40, 24, 16, 12 | 8, 6, 4 px; five subjects in landscape, then the same in iso.*

The handoff (splats at >= 14 px, glyphs below) does not pop between 16 and 12 px: both draw
legs as 1-px runs and both carry the same zones. At 4-6 px the glyph is a 2-3 px body, a head
pixel up or down and two leg pixels; elk still show a dark front and a pale rear pixel, deer are
nearly invisible on grass, which the naturalist says is true.

---

## Stage 5: poses, gaits, facings

![poses](stages/s1/poses_p1_1.png)

- **Gaits from Muybridge** (`rig.gait_pose`): walk (lateral sequence LH-LF-RH-RF, duty 0.66, the
  head nodding with each forefoot), trot (diagonals, duty 0.42, the elk's floating trot with the
  forefeet lifted high and the head up), transverse gallop (duty 0.3, spine flexing between the
  gathered and extended suspensions), bound and stot for deer. Feet follow a stance/swing
  trajectory; IK does the rest, so the carpus folds on its own in the swing.
- **Postures** from the naturalist's table: graze (head at the grass, back line broken), alert
  (neck near vertical, ears cupped), bugle (neck forward-up, nose to the sky, antlers laid back,
  jaw open), bedded (belly on the ground, knees folded in front, hocks out behind, head up).
  The first bedded pose slid the animal down a ramp with a foreleg poking out: the belly must go
  to the ground level, and folded forelegs put their knees *forward*.
- **Facings** (sheet C): the rig is 3-D, so every yaw works. The front view needed two fixes: a
  wider stance, and a **face a shade lighter than the neck** (new material `FACE`), or the head
  vanishes into the dark chest.

![front views](stages/s1/front_col.png)

---

## Stage 6: herds

![herds](stages/s1/herd_h1.png)

`herd.py` arranges a Roosevelt cow-calf herd from the naturalist's numbers (a lead cow, ~27 %
calves, ~10 % yearlings; calves at a cow's flank; 1-2 sentinels head-up; spacing 3-10 m grazing,
2-4 m alert, 3-6 m in file) and composites it on the meadow at any px/m and camera elevation,
shadows first, bodies far to near. At 3 px/m the file on a trail is a dotted line; at 1.6 px/m
the herd is a scatter of pale-rumped dots, which is the photos' far read.

---

        # legs stiff, near vertical and bunched under it (legs never lengthen)

Bash: Deer: longer legs, black tail, forehead V, summer coat
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/deer-elk/src && python3 - <<'EOF'
import re
r=open('rig.py').read()
i=r.index('DEER_TORSO = ['); j=r.index(']',i)
def f(m):
    x,zt,zb,hw=[float(v) for v in m.groups()]
    return '(%s, %s, %.3f, %s)'%(m.group(1),m.group(2),zb+0.05*max(0,1-abs(x-0.1)/0.7),m.group(4))
blk=re.sub(r'\((-?\d+\.\d+), (-?\d+\.\d+), (-?\d+\.\d+), (-?\d+\.\d+)\)',f,r[i:j])
r=r[:i]+blk+r[j:]
r=r.replace("fore_attach=(0.26, 0.56, 0.095), hind_attach=(-0.37, 0.70, 0.11)","fore_attach=(0.26, 0.60, 0.095), hind_attach=(-0.37, 0.72, 0.11)")
r=r.replace('''    torso.rb = hw''','''    torso.rb = hw
    torso.tail_up = pose.tail_up''')
open('rig.py','w').write(r)
c=open('coat.py').read()
c=c.replace('''            out[(ex * ex + ez * ez < 1.0) & (np.abs(sth) < 0.75)] = PALE   # the small white rump under the tail''','''            if getattr(tb, "tail_up", 0) > 0.5:
                # the white under the tail shows only when the tail is flagged (a blacktail has no
                # white rump patch: that is the mule deer's mark)
                out[(ex * ex + ez * ez < 1.0) & (np.abs(sth) < 0.75)] = PALE''')
c=c.replace('''            out[:] = TAILTOP
            out[cth < -0.1] = PALE''','''            # black on the outside (the dorsal side faces back when the tail hangs), white beneath
            out[:] = TAILTOP
            out[cth > 0.35] = PALE''')
c=c.replace('''            out[(u > 0.1) & (u < 0.5) & (cth > 0.75) & (ind.sex == "m")] = DARK''','''            out[(u > 0.08) & (u < 0.55) & (cth > (0.6 if ind.sex == "m" else 0.8))] = DARK   # forehead V''')
open('coat.py','w').write(c)
p=open('palette.py').read()
p=p.replace('''    ("deer", "f"): {rig.BODY: ((98, 50, 26), (214, 132, 72)), rig.LEG: ((92, 52, 30), (206, 134, 80)),''','''    ("deer", "f"): {rig.BODY: ((96, 60, 36), (200, 146, 96)), rig.LEG: ((90, 60, 40), (192, 144, 100)),''')
open('palette.py','w').write(p)
EOF

Bash: Antler economy by size; leaning glyph antlers
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/deer-elk/src && python3 - <<'EOF'
s=open('paint.py').read()
s=s.replace('''    tubes, sp = rig.build(ind, pose)''','''    tubes, sp = rig.build(ind, pose)
    # antler economy: a tangle of 1-px tines is scribble below ~30 px. Keep the beams and the
    # long tines that make the silhouette (trez, 4th); the brow tine comes back at >= 20 px.
    if size_px < 30:
        drop = {"0", "1"} if size_px < 20 else {"1"}
        drop |= {"4"} if size_px < 24 else set()
        tubes = [t for t in tubes if not (t.name.startswith("tine") and t.name[-1] in drop)]''')
open('paint.py','w').write(s)
m=open('micro.py').read()
old=m[m.index("        if size_px >= 8:\n            for bt in beams"):m.index("        elif ind.species == \"elk\" and size_px >= 6:")]
new='''        if size_px >= 8:
            # the glyph antler leans back over the shoulders (a vertical run reads as a horn or a
            # pole): the beam's tip is swung 25 deg further back about its base, and at >= 10 px
            # one tine points forward off its middle, so it is a branching thing, not a spike
            for bt in beams[:1] if size_px < 11 else beams[:2]:
                pts = P(bt.c[[0, len(bt.c) // 2, -1]])
                base = pts[0]
                back = -1.0 if front_right else 1.0
                ang = np.radians(25) * back
                ca, sa = np.cos(ang), np.sin(ang)
                def swing(p):
                    d = p - base
                    return base + np.array([ca * d[0] + sa * d[1] * -1, sa * d[0] + ca * d[1]]) if False else base + np.array([d[0] * ca - d[1] * sa, d[0] * sa + d[1] * ca])
                p1, p2 = swing(pts[1]), swing(pts[2])
                _line(mat, step, part, base, p1, am, 1, ti, overwrite=False)
                _line(mat, step, part, p1, p2, am, 1, ti, overwrite=False)
                if size_px >= 10:
                    fwd = np.array([1.0 if front_right else -1.0, -0.6])
                    tl = max(1.5, size_px / 8)
                    _line(mat, step, part, p1, p1 + fwd / np.linalg.norm(fwd) * tl, am, 1, ti, overwrite=False)
'''
m=m.replace(old,new)
open('micro.py','w').write(m)
EOF

"""Animated gait cycles (in place, as a sprite would be) and a herd filing along a trail.

    nice -n 10 python3 anim.py          -> sheets/anim_*.gif
"""
import os, sys
os.environ.setdefault("OPENBLAS_NUM_THREADS", "1")
import numpy as np
from PIL import Image
from multiprocessing import Pool
import rig, paint, herd
from render import View

OUT = os.path.join(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)), "..", "sheets")


def frame(args):
    spn, sex, age, gait, ph, size, yaw, elev, hour = args
    ind = rig.Individual(spn, sex, age, "autumn", 3)
    R, _, k = paint.paint_index(ind, gait, ph, View(yaw=yaw, elev=elev, sun_az=-150, sun_el=35), size_px=size)
    import palette as P
    tab = P.palette(ind, hour)
    on = R.mat >= 0
    rgb = np.zeros(R.mat.shape + (3,), np.uint8)
    rgb[on] = tab[R.mat[on], R.step[on]]
    return rgb, on, R.ground_shadow > 0.45, R.origin


def cycle(pool, name, subj, gait, n=12, size=48, yaw=-14, elev=8, hour="morning", Z=4):
    jobs = [subj + (gait, i / n, size, yaw, elev, hour) for i in range(n)]
    fr = pool.map(frame, jobs)
    ox = max(f[3][0] for f in fr)
    oy = max(f[3][1] for f in fr)
    W = int(max(ox - f[3][0] + f[1].shape[1] for f in fr)) + 4
    H = int(max(oy - f[3][1] + f[1].shape[0] for f in fr)) + 4
    import palette as P, ground as G
    tab = P.palette(rig.Individual(), hour)
    g = G.meadow((H, W), size / 2.4, seed=2)
    bg = tab[P.GROUND][g]
    gsh = tab[P.GSHADOW][np.clip(g - 1, 0, 5)]
    imgs = []
    for rgb, on, sh, (fx, fy) in fr:
        img = bg.copy()
        x0, y0 = int(round(ox - fx)) + 2, int(round(oy - fy)) + 2
        h, w = on.shape
        sl = (slice(y0, y0 + h), slice(x0, x0 + w))
        img[sl][sh] = gsh[sl][sh]
        img[sl][on] = rgb[on]
        imgs.append(Image.fromarray(img.repeat(Z, 0).repeat(Z, 1)))
    dur = {"walk": 110, "trot": 70, "gallop": 55, "bound": 60, "stot": 70}.get(gait, 90)
    imgs[0].save(os.path.join(OUT, f"anim_{name}.gif"), save_all=True, append_images=imgs[1:], duration=dur, loop=0)
    print(name)


def herd_file(pool, n=24, frames=24, k=4.0, W=260, H=130, Z=3):
    mem0 = herd.elk_herd("file", n, seed=7, heading=6)
    imgs = []
    speed = 1.3            # m/s walking; a walk stride cycle ~1.1 s
    for f in range(frames):
        t = f * 0.09
        mem = []
        for m in mem0:
            hd = np.radians(m.heading)
            mem.append(herd.Member(m.ind, m.gait, (m.phase + t / 1.1) % 1.0, m.x + speed * t * np.cos(hd), m.y + speed * t * np.sin(hd), m.heading))
        c = (np.mean([m.x for m in mem0]) + 1.0, np.mean([m.y for m in mem0]))
        img = herd.scene(mem, k, W, H, elev=30, hour="golden", sun_az=150, seed=5, pool=pool, centre=c)
        imgs.append(Image.fromarray(img.repeat(Z, 0).repeat(Z, 1)))
    imgs[0].save(os.path.join(OUT, "anim_herd_file_golden.gif"), save_all=True, append_images=imgs[1:], duration=90, loop=0)
    print("herd")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    with Pool(4) as pool:
        cycle(pool, "elk_cow_walk_48", ("elk", "f", "adult"), "walk")
        cycle(pool, "elk_cow_trot_48", ("elk", "f", "adult"), "trot")
        cycle(pool, "elk_bull_gallop_48", ("elk", "m", "adult"), "gallop")
        cycle(pool, "deer_doe_bound_40", ("deer", "f", "adult"), "bound", size=40)
        cycle(pool, "deer_buck_stot_40", ("deer", "m", "adult"), "stot", size=40)
        cycle(pool, "elk_cow_walk_16", ("elk", "f", "adult"), "walk", size=16, Z=8)
        cycle(pool, "elk_bull_trot_iso_24", ("elk", "m", "adult"), "trot", size=24, yaw=-35, elev=30, Z=6)
        herd_file(pool)
Bash: Render gait and herd GIFs
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/deer-elk/src && nice -n 10 python3 anim.py 2>&1 | tail -10

85. elements/channel/out/s5_low_dusk_4x.png

Write: elements/deer-elk/src/context.py
"""In context: elk on other students' paintings.

1. The channel student's dusk gravel bar (elements/channel/out/s5_low_dusk_4x.png, read at
   native 256x144): the naturalist's late-September scene, a bull bugling on a gravel bar at
   first/last light with cows scattered behind, one cow in the shallows. Back-lit (the bright sky
   is behind them), so the rim rule carries the figures; the one in the water gets a mirror
   with a compressed range (SYNTHESIS: lights drop ~13 L*, darks stay, a cool tint, dash rows).
2. The aerial student's river valley (aerial/plates/river_valley_iso_native.png, 320x180): a
   herd in the meadow by the river seen from the air, where an elk is 1-3 px (the dot tier).

    nice -n 10 python3 context.py
"""
import os
os.environ.setdefault("OPENBLAS_NUM_THREADS", "1")
import numpy as np
from PIL import Image
import rig, paint, palette as P, render
from render import View

HERE = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
OUT = os.path.join(HERE, "..", "stages", "context")
STUDIO = "~/work/pixelart-studies"


def sprite(ind, gait, phase, yaw, elev, k, hour, sun_az, sun_el, depth=0.0, haze=None):
    size = 1.62 * ind.spec().H * k
    render.SHADOW_REACH = 1.2
    R, _, _ = paint.paint_index(ind, gait, phase, View(yaw=yaw, elev=elev, sun_az=sun_az, sun_el=sun_el), size_px=size)
    tab = P.palette(ind, hour, depth, "autumn", haze=haze)
    on = R.mat >= 0
    rgb = np.zeros(R.mat.shape + (3,), np.uint8)
    rgb[on] = tab[R.mat[on], R.step[on]]
    return rgb, on, R.ground_shadow > 0.45, R.origin, R


def darken(c, f, tint=(60, 50, 90), t=0.25):
    c = c.astype(float) * f
    return np.clip(c * (1 - t) + np.array(tint) * t * f, 0, 255).astype(np.uint8)


def paste(img, rgb, on, sh, origin, x, y, shadow_col=None, water=None):
    H, W, _ = img.shape
    ox, oy = origin
    x0, y0 = int(round(x - ox)), int(round(y - oy))
    h, w = on.shape
    ys, xs = np.nonzero(sh)
    Y, X = ys + y0, xs + x0
    ok = (Y >= 0) & (Y < H) & (X >= 0) & (X < W)
    img[Y[ok], X[ok]] = darken(img[Y[ok], X[ok]], 0.62)
    if water is not None:
        # the mirror: flip the sprite about its ground row, compress the range toward the water
        gr = int(round(oy))
        ys, xs = np.nonzero(on)
        Ym = 2 * gr - ys + y0 + 1
        Xm = xs + x0
        ok = (Ym >= 0) & (Ym < H) & (Xm >= 0) & (Xm < W)
        ok &= water[np.clip(Ym, 0, H - 1), np.clip(Xm, 0, W - 1)]
        src = rgb[ys[ok], xs[ok]].astype(float)
        dst = img[Ym[ok], Xm[ok]].astype(float)
        refl = src * 0.72 + dst * 0.28          # lights drop, darks hold, the water's tint comes in
        # horizontal dash rows break the mirror every few rows
        dash = ((Ym[ok] + (Xm[ok] // 3)) % 4) == 0
        refl[dash] = dst[dash] * 0.85 + src[dash] * 0.15
        img[Ym[ok], Xm[ok]] = np.clip(refl, 0, 255).astype(np.uint8)
    ys, xs = np.nonzero(on)
    Y, X = ys + y0, xs + x0
    ok = (Y >= 0) & (Y < H) & (X >= 0) & (X < W)
    img[Y[ok], X[ok]] = rgb[ys[ok], xs[ok]]


def dusk_gravel_bar():
    bg = np.array(Image.open(f"{STUDIO}/elements/channel/out/s5_low_dusk_4x.png").convert("RGB"))[::4, ::4].copy()
    H, W, _ = bg.shape
    # water mask: the channel is the light violet-grey band; read it off the image by colour
    b = bg.astype(int)
    water = (b[..., 2] > 150) & (b[..., 0] > 150) & (np.arange(H)[:, None] > 60)
    sky = tuple(int(v) for v in bg[20, W // 2])
    hour, sun_az, sun_el = "dusk", 95.0, 5.0     # the glow is behind them, a little to the right

    def k_at(y):                                  # perspective read off the gravel: px per metre
        return max(0.8, 1.0 + (y - 50) * 0.13)
    cast = []
    rng = np.random.default_rng(3)
    # cows scattered on the far bar, the bull nearer, one cow walking in the shallows
    for i, (x, y) in enumerate([(30, 64), (46, 62), (70, 66), (96, 63), (120, 68), (150, 64), (20, 70), (84, 71)]):
        ind = rig.Individual("elk", "f" if i != 5 else "m", "adult" if i != 3 else "young", "autumn", 40 + i)
        g = ["graze", "graze", "walk", "graze", "alert", "alert", "graze", "walk"][i]
        cast.append((y, ind, g, rng.random(), rng.uniform(-40, 40) + (0 if rng.random() < 0.6 else 180), x))
    cast.append((92, rig.Individual("elk", "m", "adult", "autumn", 7), "bugle", 0.0, -25.0, 62))
    cast.append((112, rig.Individual("elk", "f", "adult", "autumn", 8), "walk", 0.35, -160.0, 205))
    img = bg.copy()
    for y, ind, g, ph, yaw, x in sorted(cast, key=lambda c: c[0]):
        k = k_at(y)
        depth = float(np.clip((80 - y) / 40, 0, 0.7))
        rgb, on, sh, org, R = sprite(ind, g, ph, yaw, 6.0, k, hour, sun_az, sun_el, depth=depth, haze=sky)
        paste(img, rgb, on, sh, org, x, y, water=water if water[min(y + 1, H - 1), x] else None)
    os.makedirs(OUT, exist_ok=True)
    Image.fromarray(img).save(os.path.join(OUT, "dusk_gravel_bar_1x.png"))
    Image.fromarray(img.repeat(4, 0).repeat(4, 1)).save(os.path.join(OUT, "dusk_gravel_bar_4x.png"))
    # detail crop around the bull at 8x
    Image.fromarray(img[70:110, 30:110].repeat(8, 0).repeat(8, 1)).save(os.path.join(OUT, "dusk_gravel_bar_bull_8x.png"))


def dot(img, x, y, ind, heading, rng):
    """The dot tier (an elk 1-3 px long): a pale body pixel, a dark front pixel toward the head,
    and a 1-px shadow under it; calves are a single darker pixel."""
    H, W, _ = img.shape
    body = np.array((214, 186, 146)) if ind.species == "elk" else np.array((150, 128, 100))
    dark = np.array((88, 64, 46))
    fx = 1 if np.cos(np.radians(heading)) >= 0 else -1
    if not (0 <= x < W - 2 and 1 <= y < H - 1):
        return
    img[y + 1, x] = darken(img[y + 1, x][None], 0.7)[0]
    if ind.age == "young":
        img[y, x] = (body * 0.8).astype(np.uint8)
        return
    img[y, x] = body
    if 0 <= x + fx < W:
        img[y, x + fx] = dark


def aerial_valley():
    bg = np.array(Image.open(f"{STUDIO}/aerial/plates/river_valley_iso_native.png").convert("RGB")).copy()
    import herd
    rng = np.random.default_rng(5)
    img = bg.copy()
    # meadow pixels: the valley floor's light greens (read by colour)
    b = bg.astype(int)
    meadow = (b[..., 1] > 130) & (b[..., 1] > b[..., 0] + 20) & (b[..., 2] < 110)
    mem = herd.elk_herd("graze", 30, seed=4, heading=-20)
    # place the herd at a meadow point near the river; ~0.5 px per metre (a crown ~ 5 px)
    cy, cx = 104, 150
    for m in mem:
        x = int(round(cx + m.x * 0.55))
        y = int(round(cy - m.y * 0.55 * 0.5))
        if meadow[np.clip(y, 0, bg.shape[0] - 1), np.clip(x, 0, bg.shape[1] - 1)]:
            dot(img, x, y, m.ind, m.heading, rng)
    # a file on a trail to the river further up
    mem2 = herd.elk_herd("file", 14, seed=9, heading=60)
    for m in mem2:
        x = int(round(200 + m.x * 0.55)); y = int(round(60 - m.y * 0.55 * 0.5))
        if 0 <= y < bg.shape[0] and 0 <= x < bg.shape[1] and meadow[y, x]:
            dot(img, x, y, m.ind, m.heading, rng)
    os.makedirs(OUT, exist_ok=True)
    Image.fromarray(img.repeat(4, 0).repeat(4, 1)).save(os.path.join(OUT, "aerial_valley_herd_4x.png"))
    Image.fromarray(img[80:130, 110:200].repeat(10, 0).repeat(10, 1)).save(os.path.join(OUT, "aerial_valley_herd_crop_10x.png"))


if __name__ == "__main__":
    dusk_gravel_bar()
    aerial_valley()

Bash: Sparser single-pixel dot tier for aerial herd
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/deer-elk/src && python3 - <<'EOF'
c=open('context.py').read()
old=c[c.index("def dot(img, x, y, ind, heading, rng):"):c.index("def aerial_valley():")]
new='''def dot(img, x, y, ind, heading, rng, taken):
    """The dot tier (an elk ~1.3 px long at 0.55 px/m): one pale pixel, its shadow one pixel
    down-right in the grass's own dark, and nothing else. Calves are a single mid-brown pixel.
    Dots never touch: a herd at this scale is a scatter of pale specks, not a smudge
    (the first version drew a dark front pixel too and the herd became brown scribble)."""
    H, W, _ = img.shape
    if not (0 <= x < W - 1 and 0 <= y < H - 1) or taken[max(0, y - 1):y + 2, max(0, x - 1):x + 2].any():
        return
    body = np.array((224, 200, 158)) if ind.age != "young" else np.array((150, 110, 80))
    img[y + 1, x + 1] = darken(img[y + 1, x + 1][None], 0.66)[0]
    img[y, x] = body
    taken[y, x] = True


'''
c=c.replace(old,new)
c=c.replace("    mem = herd.elk_herd(\"graze\", 30, seed=4, heading=-20)","    taken = np.zeros(bg.shape[:2], bool)\n    mem = herd.elk_herd(\"graze\", 30, seed=4, heading=-20)")
c=c.replace("dot(img, x, y, m.ind, m.heading, rng)","dot(img, x, y, m.ind, m.heading, rng, taken)")
open('context.py','w').write(c)
EOF
nice -n 10 python3 -c "

Bash: Glyph study sheet on flat ground
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/deer-elk/src && cat > glyphs.py <<'EOF'
"""Glyph study: the small painter's output on a flat ground at 12x, for pixel-by-pixel judging."""
import numpy as np, sys
from PIL import Image
import rig, paint, palette as P
from render import View
tag = sys.argv[1]
SIZES = [13, 12, 10, 8, 7, 6, 5, 4]
subs = [('elk', 'f', 'adult', 'stand', 0), ('elk', 'f', 'adult', 'graze', 0), ('elk', 'f', 'adult', 'walk', .3), ('elk', 'm', 'adult', 'stand', 0),
        ('elk', 'm', 'adult', 'trot', .3), ('elk', 'f', 'young', 'walk', .5), ('deer', 'f', 'adult', 'alert', 0), ('deer', 'm', 'adult', 'bound', .3), ('elk', 'f', 'adult', 'lie', 0)]
rows = []
for elev, yaw in ((6, -12), (30, -35)):
    for spn, sex, age, g, ph in subs:
        ind = rig.Individual(spn, sex, age, 'autumn', 3)
        tab = P.palette(ind, 'morning', 0, 'summer')
        cells = []
        for L in SIZES:
            R, _, k = paint.paint_index(ind, g, ph, View(yaw=yaw, elev=elev, sun_az=-150, sun_el=35), size_px=L)
            img = np.zeros(R.mat.shape + (3,), np.uint8); img[:] = tab[P.GROUND][3]
            img[R.ground_shadow > 0.45] = tab[P.GSHADOW][2]
            on = R.mat >= 0
            img[on] = tab[R.mat[on], R.step[on]]
            cells.append(img)
        rows.append(cells)
Z = 10
tiles = []
for cells in rows:
    hh = max(c.shape[0] for c in cells); t = np.full((hh, sum(c.shape[1] + 2 for c in cells), 3), 30, np.uint8); x = 0
    for c in cells:
        t[hh - c.shape[0]:, x:x + c.shape[1]] = c; x += c.shape[1] + 2
    tiles.append(t)
W = max(t.shape[1] for t in tiles)
out = np.full((sum(t.shape[0] + 2 for t in tiles), W, 3), 30, np.uint8); y = 0
for t in tiles:
    out[y:y + t.shape[0], :t.shape[1]] = t; y += t.shape[0] + 2
im = out.repeat(Z, 0).repeat(Z, 1)
Image.fromarray(im[: im.shape[0] // 2 + 20]).save(f'../stages/s1/glyphs_{tag}_side.png')
Image.fromarray(im[im.shape[0] // 2 - 20:]).save(f'../stages/s1/glyphs_{tag}_iso.png')
EOF
nice python3 glyphs.py g1

m=open('micro.py').read()
m=m.replace('''    maxrows = max(minrows, int(round(ncol / 2.4)))''','''    # the far read is a body mass about as deep as the legs are long: at glyph sizes that is
    # ~0.55 of the shoulder height in rows (the projected torso rounds to 2 rows at 8 px and the
    # glyph became a table on stilts beside the splat renderer's 3-row body at 10 px)
    hpx = size_px / 1.62 * (1.0 if ind.age != "young" else 0.85)
    target = max(minrows, int(round(0.55 * hpx)))
    if not getattr(ind, "_lying", False):
        for cx, (t, b) in list(cols.items()):
            if b - t < target:
                cols[cx] = (b - target, b)
    maxrows = max(minrows, target, int(round(ncol / 2.4)))''')
open('micro.py','w').write(m)

Bash: Glyph antler for 8-16 px bulls, shared function
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/deer-elk/src && python3 - <<'EOF'
m=open('micro.py').read()
start=m.index("    # --- antlers\n    ant = ind.antler()")
end=m.index("    # --- ears: a 1-px tick up and back")
old=m[start:end]
m=m.replace(old,'''    # --- antlers
    glyph_antler(ind, tubes, P, depth, size_px, front_right, mat, step, part, ti)
''')
m=m.replace('''def paint(ind, tubes, sp, view, k, size_px):''','''def glyph_antler(ind, tubes, P, depth, size_px, front_right, mat, step, part, ti, max_h=None):
    """The small antler: beams leaning back ~45 deg over the neck (a vertical run reads as a
    horn, a pole, a giraffe's neck), no taller than the body is deep, with one tine pointing
    forward off each beam's middle at >= 10 px; a single dark pixel above the head at 6-7 px."""
    ant = ind.antler()
    if not ant:
        return
    am = rig.ANTLER if ant != "velvet" else rig.VELVET
    beams = [t for t in tubes if t.name.startswith(("beam", "ant"))]
    if not beams:
        return
    beams.sort(key=lambda t: depth(t.c.mean(0)))
    back = -1.0 if front_right else 1.0
    if size_px >= 8:
        for bt in beams[:1] if size_px < 11 else beams[:2]:
            pts = P(bt.c[[0, len(bt.c) // 2, -1]])
            base = pts[0]
            L = np.linalg.norm(pts[2] - base)
            if max_h is not None:
                L = min(L, max_h)
            L = max(L, 2.5)
            a = np.radians(48)
            tip = base + np.array([back * np.sin(a), -np.cos(a)]) * L
            mid = base + (tip - base) * 0.5 + np.array([back * 0.3, 0])
            _line(mat, step, part, base, mid, am, 1, ti, overwrite=False)
            _line(mat, step, part, mid, tip, am, 1, ti, overwrite=False)
            if size_px >= 10:
                fwd = np.array([-back, -0.8])
                fwd /= np.linalg.norm(fwd)
                _line(mat, step, part, mid, mid + fwd * max(1.5, L * 0.45), am, 1, ti, overwrite=False)
    elif ind.species == "elk" and size_px >= 6:
        top = P(beams[0].c[0]) + np.array([back * 1.0, -1.0])
        _put(mat, step, part, int(round(top[0])), int(round(top[1])), am, 1, ti, overwrite=False)


def paint(ind, tubes, sp, view, k, size_px):''')
open('micro.py','w').write(m)
s=open('paint.py').read()
s=s.replace('''    view = replace(view, ss=int(np.clip(round(260 / size_px), 3, 12)))
    R = render.rasterize(tubes, ind, view, k)''','''    view = replace(view, ss=int(np.clip(round(260 / size_px), 3, 12)))
    # 10-16 px: the splat renderer's antlers are a bar with a crook (a llama's neck); the glyph
    # antler takes over, drawn after the hide
    glyph_ant = size_px < 16 and ind.antler() is not None
    all_tubes = tubes
    if glyph_ant:
        tubes = [t for t in tubes if not t.name.startswith(("beam", "tine", "ant", "fork", "brow"))]
    R = render.rasterize(tubes, ind, view, k)''')
s=s.replace('''    R.k = k
    R = contact_shadow(R, tubes, view, k)
    import hide, fringe''','''    R.k = k
    R = contact_shadow(R, tubes, view, k)
    import hide, fringe
    R._glyph_ant = glyph_ant
    R._all_tubes = all_tubes''')
s=s.replace('''            R = fringe.hang(R, mp, rig.DARK, size_px, ind.seed, density=0.25, maxlen=1)
    return R, tubes, k''','''            R = fringe.hang(R, mp, rig.DARK, size_px, ind.seed, density=0.25, maxlen=1)
    if glyph_ant:
        import micro
        fv = render.view_xf(view)
        ox, oy = R.origin

        def P(p):
            sx, sv, d = fv(np.asarray(p))
            return np.stack([sx * k + ox, oy - sv * k], -1)
        tor = [t for t in all_tubes if t.name == "torso"][0]
        front_right = P(tor.c[-1])[0] > P(tor.c[0])[0]
        body_depth = 0.5 * sp.H * k * 1.1
        micro.glyph_antler(ind, all_tubes, P, lambda p: fv(np.asarray(p))[2], size_px, front_right,
                           R.mat, R.step, R.part, 0, max_h=body_depth)
    return R, tubes, k''')
open('paint.py','w').write(s)
EOF

Bash: Backlit glyphs, 2-px aerial dots; rerender
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/deer-elk/src && python3 - <<'EOF'
m=open('micro.py').read()
m=m.replace('''    Rall.mat = mat
    Rall.step = step''','''    # back light: small figures against a low sun are dark silhouettes with the sun's colour
    # along the top edge (the dusk scene's far herd was lit like noon: khaki blobs)
    Lw = render.sun_vec(view.sun_az, view.sun_el)
    if Lw[1] > 0.4 and view.diffuse < 0.5:
        on_ = mat >= 0
        step[on_] = np.minimum(step[on_], 1)
        top_ = on_ & ~np.vstack([np.zeros((1, W), bool), on_[:-1]])
        brk = ((np.arange(W)[None, :] * 73 + np.arange(H)[:, None] * 151) % 7) == 0
        rimp = top_ & ~brk & ~np.isin(mat, [rig.ANTLER, rig.VELVET])
        mat[rimp] = 19
        step[rimp] = 4
    Rall.mat = mat
    Rall.step = step''')
open('micro.py','w').write(m)
c=open('context.py').read()
old=c[c.index("def dot(img, x, y, ind, heading, rng, taken):"):c.index("def aerial_valley():")]
new='''def dot(img, x, y, ind, heading, rng, taken):
    """The dot tier (an elk ~1.3 px long at 0.55 px/m): two pixels, the pale rump behind and the
    dark front toward the head, and a 1-px shadow tick on the shadow side. Calves are a single
    mid-brown pixel. Dots never touch. (Single cream pixels read as daisies or gravel; a dark
    front and pale back, the naturalist's far read, is what says animal.)"""
    H, W, _ = img.shape
    fx = 1 if np.cos(np.radians(heading)) >= 0 else -1
    xs = [x, x + fx]
    if not (1 <= min(xs) and max(xs) < W - 1 and 1 <= y < H - 1) or taken[y - 1:y + 2, min(xs) - 1:max(xs) + 2].any():
        return
    pale = np.array((226, 204, 162))
    dark = np.array((96, 70, 50))
    img[y + 1, x + 1] = darken(img[y + 1, x + 1][None], 0.66)[0]
    if ind.age == "young":
        img[y, x] = np.array((140, 100, 72))
        taken[y, x] = True
        return
    img[y, x] = pale
    img[y, x + fx] = dark
    taken[y, x] = taken[y, x + fx] = True


'''
c=c.replace(old,new)
open('context.py','w').write(c)
EOF

Write: elements/deer-elk/LESSONS.md
# Roosevelt elk and black-tailed deer, after Mark Ferrari: lessons

A student's log. Subject: Roosevelt elk (bull, cow, yearling, calf) and Columbian black-tailed
deer (buck, doe, fawn), as individuals and as herds, from a 60 px hero animal down to a 4 px
figure and a 2 px dot, in the landscape view (camera 6-10 deg) and the 30 deg iso view.

Everything here is made by the programs in `src/`. Stage images are in `stages/`, parameter
sheets and animations in `sheets/`, photos in `refs/` (fetched with the studio's imgfetch;
licences in each `sources.tsv`) plus the naturalist's `~/work/naturalist/animals/photos/`.



cd src
nice -n 10 python3 sheets.py            # sheets A-H (~5 min, 4 processes)
nice -n 10 python3 anim.py              # gait and herd GIFs
nice -n 10 python3 context.py           # the two context pieces
python3 s0_copy.py                      # the stage-0 master copy




The technique as code: one call paints one animal, one more paints a herd.



import rig, paint, herd
from render import View
ind = rig.Individual(&quot;elk&quot;, &quot;m&quot;, &quot;adult&quot;, season=&quot;autumn&quot;, seed=2)     # species, sex, age, season, seed
R, tubes, k = paint.paint_index(ind, gait=&quot;trot&quot;, phase=0.3,           # stand graze walk trot gallop
                                view=View(yaw=-14, elev=8,              #   alert bugle lie bound stot
                                          sun_az=-150, sun_el=30, diffuse=0),
                                size_px=48)                             # body length in px
rgb = paint.colour(R, ind, hour=&quot;golden&quot;, ground=&quot;autumn&quot;, depth=0.0)   # the palette is the light
img = herd.scene(herd.elk_herd(&quot;graze&quot;, 26, seed=7), k=6, W=320, H=180, elev=30, hour=&quot;morning&quot;)




`R.mat` / `R.step` are the index canvas (material, step 0-5); the palette maps them to colour for
an hour, season and depth, so one canvas serves every time of day.

| module | what it is |
|---|---|
| `rig.py` | the animal: species proportions (torso lofted from photo stations), sex/age/season/seed variation, poses and gaits (IK legs), antlers |
| `render.py` | tubes -> supersampled surface splats -> z-buffer, light-space shadow, pixel reduction; thin parts redrawn as clean runs |
| `coat.py` | hide regions in pose-free body coordinates: the elk's cape, rump patch, dark belly, face; deer throat, forehead, black tail, ears; fawn spots |
| `hide.py` | pixel rules from the Ferrari copy: plane flattening, edge bands, lit edges, back-light rim, hair-dash dither |
| `fringe.py` | fur edges: interlocking teeth between hide colours, the bull's hanging mane |
| `micro.py` | the glyph painter under 10 px, and the small antler used up to 16 px |
| `palette.py` | (material, step) -> colour per coat, hour, season and depth |
| `herd.py` | herd arrangements from the naturalist's guide, and the compositor |
| `ground.py` | a calm meadow to stand on (grass-lod owns grass; this is only a backdrop) |
| `sheets.py`, `anim.py`, `context.py` | the deliverables |
| `board.py`, `sizes*.py`, `ladder.py`, `glyphs.py`, `triptych.py`, `poses.py`, `facings.py` | the comparison tools used at each stage |

---

## Stage 0: a master copy, because Ferrari never painted an elk

There is no animal in any Ferrari scene in the studio (`pedagogy/master-copy/ref/lw`,
`shared/refs`). The closest passage to "a warm-coloured, rounded, sunlit mass the size of a
60-px elk's body" is the orange boulder in front of the stone gate in *V19* (native
(238,255)-(280,290)). I copied it from its index map (`src/s0_copy.py`, with the loader borrowed
from the willow student).

![boulder copy](stages/s0/s0_boulder_copy_8x.png)

*Left: Ferrari. Right: my copy, from his silhouette and palette.*

What reading his indices showed:

- **The lit face is one gradient, not a dome.** Almost the whole face is a mix of two adjacent
  steps (`c` and `f`), with pure `f` only on the brightest margin and pure `c` where it turns.
  The mix is a 2x2 Bayer (DPaint) fill: rows alternate `cccc` / `fcfc` at 25 %, and it becomes a
  checker at 50 %. I fitted the f-density as a *linear* function of position: it rises 3.6 % per
  px to the right and reproduces 81 % of his face pixels exactly. A dome model got 50 %.
- **The form turns only at the edges**, in 1-2 px bands: a dull rose `a` one step down, then `8`,
  a **saturated** red-brown on the outermost shadow-side pixels. The reflected colour is more
  saturated than the lit ones, so my palettes boost chroma on step 2 by 30 %.
- **Lost and found edges.** Against the lighter snow the lit face simply stops, with no outline.
  Against the dark, the shadow side has its saturated rim.

Overall the copy matches 68 % of his pixels (81 % on the face); the misses are at the edge bands.
This stage gave me `hide.py`: Bayer gradients on lit planes, a flattened interior, the
shadow-edge band and the saturated reflected step. Not all of it transferred: a barrel is not a
facet, and hair is not rock (stage 3).

---

## Stage 1: the silhouette, in notan and greyscale, against photos

The naturalist's first principle: behaviour is legible in silhouette, and at a distance the
silhouette is all you have. What carries it is the ratio of body to leg to neck, the head height
against the back line, the one high-contrast patch, and how the animal sits with the others.

### How the form is built (`rig.py`)

- **The torso is not a capsule.** It is lofted from 13 stations (x, back height, belly height,
  half-width) read off side-view photos:
  - the level back, with the withers a touch above the croup;
  - the deep chest ahead of the forelegs (the brisket);
  - the belly rising into the flank tuck, and the ham dropping behind it;
  - the croup sloping to the tail root, and the rounded buttock.
- **The neck is a wedge.** It is very deep where it leaves the withers and the brisket, tapers
  fast, then runs nearly parallel to the throat latch. Its top line starts at the withers, so
  there is no notch between back and neck. The bull's mane is a separate tube hanging under it.
- **Legs follow anatomy.**
  - Foreleg: a forearm deep fore-and-aft at the top (muscle), a carpus knob, a slim flat-sided
    cannon, a fetlock knob, a pastern and a small dark wedge of a hoof.
  - Hind leg: it starts as the ham, whose rear edge continues the buttock, and tapers to the
    hock. The point of the hock sticks out behind, and a long cannon follows.
  - IK places each hoof. Forelegs bend forward at the carpus, hind legs back at the hock.
- **Antlers are fixed to the skull**, so their angle in the world follows the head's pitch.
  - The beam sweeps up and back and spreads wide.
  - Each tine leaves the beam turned forward by an angle measured from the beam itself (the brow
    tine about 95 deg, the upper ones about 50) and curls back toward it.
  - A Roosevelt bull crowns at the top. A blacktail buck carries a small dichotomous fork.

### The comparisons that shaped it

![notan, early](stages/s1/board_notan1.png)
![notan, later](stages/s1/board_notan5.png)

*Photo | mine, at the same shoulder height and ground line: a cow (alert), a bull (standing),
Muybridge's elk trotting (plate 692, frame 15), and a blacktail buck walking. First image:
early; second: after the fixes below.*

What the photos corrected, in order. Each one was visible in the notan and invisible in my head.

1. **The torso was 1.4 shoulder heights long.** The photos measure 1.05-1.3 H: the Muybridge bull
   about 1.3, a Roosevelt cow 1.05. A long torso on straight legs is what made the old capsule rig
   read as a horse. The torso is now about 1.1 H for elk and 1.0 H for deer.
2. **The neck left the chest from the wrong place.** It must rise from the withers on top and
   from the brisket below, as one wedge. A tube stuck on the front of a box reads as a llama.
3. **The rump was a box end.** The croup slopes and the buttock rounds down into the hams, whose
   rear edge runs down to the hock.
4. **The legs were 50 % too thick.** An elk's cannon is about 0.035 H across, so at 60 px it is a
   1-px line. A 2-px dark leg was the heaviest mark on the animal.
5. **The hind leg was a post.** The gaskin is longer than the stifle's height allows, so the leg
   has to bend, with the hock behind the line from stifle to hoof.
6. **The antlers were wrong twice.** First they stood straight up like a hat rack; then the tines
   pointed forward like a comb. Tines must leave the beam at an angle measured from the beam.
7. **The tail pointed forward, into the rump** (a sign error, found in the last round). That is
   why the blacktail's black tail never showed.

![grey board](stages/s1/board_grey5.png)

---

## Stage 2: colour, and the far read

![colour at sizes](stages/s1/sizesc_c1.png)

*Photo reduced by area-averaging | mine, at 60, 30, 16 and 8 px (4x). An early state.*

- **Hide colours come from the photos** (`palette.py`):
  - the cow: pale greyish tan with a mid-chocolate neck;
  - the autumn bull: orange-tan, near cream in sun, with a black-brown mane;
  - a pale straw rump patch edged dark, and dark legs and belly;
  - blacktails: grey-brown in autumn and winter, tawny in summer.
  - Seasons: winter coats are greyer and duller; spring coats are dull (the moult).
  - Calves and fawns are spotted in summer and plain by September, as they are in life.
- **Regions are anatomical, not boxes** (`coat.py`, in pose-free body coordinates).
  - The elk's cape runs back over the shoulder on a diagonal, from just behind the withers to
    behind the elbow.
  - The rump patch is an oval about the tail, bordered dark on the thighs and open at the croup.
  - The elk's face (`FACE`) is a shade lighter than its neck, or seen from the front the head
    vanishes into the chest.
  - The blacktail's tail is black outside and white beneath. It shows **no white rump unless the
    tail is flagged**: a white rump is the mule deer's mark.
  - My first regions were vertical cuts at a fixed x, and they looked pasted on.
- **The far read survives.** At 16 and 8 px the reduced photo is a pale body with a darker front
  end, and so is mine. Mine is more legible than the photo, as pixel art should be.
- **Light across the form.** With the sun from the front-left the whole visible side lit to one
  step, and the first renders were flat. With the sun about 25 deg in front of side-on (azimuth
  -150 to -160) the back is lit, the side is a halftone and the belly is shaded.

---

## Stage 3: the hide at hero size (60-30 px)

![hero, flat](stages/s1/zoom_z2.png)
![hero, hide rules](stages/s1/zoom_z7.png)

- **Bayer planes and edge bands** come from stage 0 (`hide.py`). On a rock the interior is
  flattened hard toward one value, the facet. **An elk's barrel is not a facet:** with the same
  flattening its side went dead. Flattening is 0.5 on the copy and 0.2 on hide.
- **The value mapping must not saturate.** My first mapping put every surface facing up and out
  on the top step, so ten rows of flank were one value. Mapping the lit family linearly from
  n.L = 0.04 to 1 onto steps 2.8-5.15 gives a lit back, a mid flank and a halftone lower flank.
- **Hair is not rock.** A 2x2 checker on a hide reads as sandstone. `hide.hair_quant` keeps the
  order but makes the cells 1 px wide and 2 px tall, staggered per column. The mixed tone becomes
  short vertical dashes, the hair lying down the flank.
- **Fur edges** (`fringe.py`):
  - Where cape meets body, rump meets thigh, and body meets belly, the boundary interlocks in 1-px
    teeth on alternate 2-row blocks.
  - The bull's mane hangs below the neck line as irregular dark ticks, 1-3 px long.
- **Found edges for dark hide.** A dark neck or leg against grass has almost the grass's value.
  The sun-side silhouette pixel of dark hide steps up to at least the halftone, so the neck
  separates without an outline.
- **The far side sits one step down**: far limbs, far ear and far antler. The shoulder bulge
  models the lit side but may not draw its own terminator; a 1-px core line there read as a strap.
- **Antlers are drawn, not rasterized.**
  - Beams and tines are 1-px clean runs, 2 px near the base, with the shadow-side pixel a step
    darker. Beams are dark and only the tips are ivory.
  - **Economy by size:** below 30 px only the beams and the long tines remain. A tangle of 1-px
    tines is scribble.
  - Rasterized, the antlers were a blur with white blobs at the ends.
- **Head carriage.** A head held 45 deg nose-down with a long face reads as a horse or a moose.
  An elk standing carries its head about 24 deg down, trotting about 12 ("head high, nose out"),
  and galloping about 18.
- **Contact.** Under the torso there is an occlusion shadow whatever the sun (the animal must
  sit on the meadow), plus the true cast shadow from a light-space z-buffer. The cast shadow is
  kept one step lighter than the ground's darks: darker, it swallowed the legs in iso.

### Light and hour (`sheets/D_individual_x_hour.png`)

- **Back light is its own rule.** With the sun behind the animal, the edges facing the glow
  light up. This is Ferrari's V02 rim. It takes **the sun's colour**, not the hide's: a rim in the
  hide's top step at a dusk key of 0.66 was too dim to read against a glowing sky.
- **Round 2 of the critique called my rim "a neon outline"**, and it was. It now lights only
  silhouette pixels whose outward normal faces the glow (dot > 0.3), never the belly's underside,
  and it breaks every few pixels.
- **Overcast is diffuse.** The sky is the light: the value follows how much sky a surface sees,
  held close, with no cast shadow.
- **Dawn is cooler** than morning (a pink sun, a violet ambient).

![golden rim](stages/s1/golden_rim2.png)
![dusk rims](stages/s1/dusk_rim2.png)

### Honest comparison at 60 px

![cow](stages/s1/trip_t6_0.png)
![bull](stages/s1/trip_t6_1.png)
![bull trotting](stages/s1/trip_t6_2.png)
![buck](stages/s1/trip_t6_3.png)

*Photo reduced to 60 px | mine | Ferrari's boulder, for craft (6x).*

- **The trot against Muybridge is the best animal in the set** (the critic said so too): head
  forward, antlers sweeping back over the shoulders, forefoot folded.
- **The cow reads as an elk cow beside her photo**, now that her head is level.
- **Not there yet:**
  - Against the photo, the standing bull is less weighty. His shoulder and ham are lit masses
    with darker ribs between; mine is a lit barrel with hair dashes.
  - Against Ferrari, my planes are still too few. His boulder has three clear planes and an edge
    band; my barrel has a gradient.
  - The deer's summer coat and black tail are now right. Its ears and velvet antlers still smear
    into one grey knot at 60 px.

---

## Stage 4: every size, 60 px to 4 px

![blobs](stages/s1/ladder_micro1.png)

*The splat renderer from 16 to 4 px: below about 10 px, legs and antlers turn into blobs.*

A pixel artist at 8 px does not render an elk; he places it. `micro.py` is a glyph painter fed by
the same rig's keypoints:

- **Body:** per column, the torso's projected top and bottom. It is at least **0.55 of the
  shoulder height deep in rows**. The photo-true torso rounds to 2 rows at 8 px, and beside the
  splat renderer's 3-row body at 10 px the glyph was a table on stilts. Corners are knocked off
  at >= 9 px.
- **Zones:** elk have the pale rump in the rear ~18 % and the dark cape in the front ~28 %; the
  top row is in the sun.
- **Legs:** per pair, the near leg is a clean 1-px run from belly to hoof. The far leg is drawn
  only when its hoof is 2+ px from the near one (walking, running), one step darker. The hock
  appears only at >= 12 px; below that it reads as a tail curling up.
- **Neck and head:** 1-px runs. The head gets a jaw pixel and **an ear tick** at >= 8-9 px.
  Without them it was a gun barrel on a table.
- **Antler** (`micro.glyph_antler`, also used by the splat renderer up to 16 px): beams lean back
  about 45 deg over the neck, no taller than 1.5 body-depths, with one forward tine at >= 10 px.
  At 6-7 px it is a single dark pixel; below 6 px it is dropped. A vertical run read as a horn, a
  pole, or a giraffe's neck.
- **Back light:** a small figure against a low sun is a dark silhouette with the sun's colour
  along its top edge.

![glyph study, side](stages/s1/glyphs_g6_side.png)
![glyph study, iso](stages/s1/glyphs_g6_iso.png)

*The small tier on flat ground at 10x. Columns 13, 12 and 10 px are the splat renderer; 8, 7,
6, 5 and 4 px are the glyph painter. Rows: cow standing, grazing, walking; bull standing,
trotting; calf; doe; buck bounding; cow bedded.*

**The handoff moved from 14 px to 10 px** after this study. At 10-13 px the splat renderer's
masses read as a heavy-bodied animal, and the glyphs there were stick figures. Below 10 px the
glyphs are cleaner than the splats.

**Where the small tier is still weak** (honest):

- A grazing cow at 8-6 px is a block with a hook (the critic said "pig or table").
- A calf at 6-4 px is a T.
- Deer on grass at 8-4 px are close to invisible. The naturalist says that is true ("visible
  only moving or on snow"), but a game may want a little more.

![ladder](stages/s1/ladder_l2.png)

---

## Stage 5: poses, gaits, facings

![poses](stages/s1/poses_p2_0.png)

- **Gaits after Muybridge** (`rig.gait_pose`; `sheets/F_gait_cycles.png`; the GIFs):
  - walk: lateral sequence LH-LF-RH-RF, duty 0.66, the head nodding with each forefoot;
  - trot: diagonal pairs, duty 0.42; the elk's floating trot, forefeet lifted high, head up;
  - transverse gallop: duty 0.3, the spine flexing between the gathered and extended
    suspensions;
  - bound and stot for deer.
  - The feet follow stance/swing trajectories and IK does the rest, so the carpus folds by itself
    in the swing.
- **The stot was stilts:** the legs lengthened to keep the hooves on the ground. Now the whole
  animal leaves the ground, with the legs stiff, fixed in length and bunched under it, and the
  shadow left behind.
- **Postures** follow the naturalist's table:
  - graze: head at the grass, well ahead of the forefeet, so it does not merge with the legs;
  - alert: neck near vertical, ears cupped;
  - bugle: neck forward and up, nose to the sky, antlers laid back, jaw open;
  - bedded: belly on the ground, knees folded forward, hocks tucked, body spread wide, head up.
    The first bedded pose slid the animal down a ramp with a foreleg poking out.
- **Facings** (`sheets/C_individual_x_facing.png`): the rig is 3-D, so every yaw works. Front
  views needed a wider stance and the lighter face.

![front views](stages/s1/front_col.png)

---

## Stage 6: herds, and in context

`herd.py` arranges a Roosevelt cow-calf herd from the naturalist's numbers:

- a lead cow, ~27 % calves and ~10 % yearlings;
- calves at a cow's flank, and one or two sentinels head-up;
- spacing of 3-10 m grazing, 2-4 m alert, 3-6 m in file.

It composites the herd on the meadow at any px/m and camera elevation: shadows first, then
bodies far to near. At 3 px/m the file on a trail is a dotted line; at 1.6 px/m the herd is a
scatter of pale-rumped figures, the photos' far read.

![herds](sheets/G_herds.png)

**In context** (`context.py`), on other students' paintings:

![dusk gravel bar](stages/context/dusk_gravel_bar_4x.png)

- **The channel student's dusk gravel bar.** This is the naturalist's late-September scene: a
  bull on the bar at last light with cows scattered behind, and one cow in the shallows.
  - The figures are back-lit, so they are dark with a sun-coloured top rim.
  - The cow in the water has a mirror with a compressed range (lights drop, darks hold, the
    water's tint comes in, dash rows break it).
  - Scale comes from reading the perspective off the gravel, about 1 to 10 px/m.

![aerial](stages/context/aerial_valley_herd_crop_10x.png)

- **The aerial student's river valley** at native 320x180, where an elk is about 1.3 px (the dot
  tier).
  - Each animal is two pixels, a pale rump behind a dark front, with a 1-px shadow tick, and no
    two dots touch.
  - Single cream pixels read as daisies.

## Where it stands (honest)

**Good enough to hand on:**

- The rig. It is unmistakably elk or deer in silhouette at 60-10 px, in any facing, landscape or
  iso, including the gaits: the horsey capsule is gone.
- The far read: a dark front, a pale rear and the rump patch, down to a 2-px dot.
- Herd arrangement and compositing.
- The index-canvas-plus-palette structure, so hour, season and haze are palette swaps.
- The trot, the cow's head, the stot and the backlit figures in the dusk scene.

**Not there yet:**

1. **Hero-scale craft is below Ferrari.** My lit side is a gradient with hair dashes, not three
   or four designed planes with edge bands. The next step would be to model the shoulder, ribcage
   and ham as separate lit planes and to copy one of his figures at 8x (the V02 dead tree's rim
   was the closest thing to an animal silhouette I found).
2. **The standing bull lacks weight**: the neck and mane are thinner than a September Roosevelt
   bull's.
3. **The small tier has weak cells**: grazing at 8-6 px, calves at 6-4 px, deer on grass.
4. **Rims** are now directional and broken, but at golden hour they could still be richer on the
   thin forms (ears, antler tips, belly fringe), as the critic asked.
5. **Iso cast shadows** are physically placed; in herds at 4 px/m they are larger than the
   animals, and a stylized shorter shadow might read better.
6. **Performance:** a 60-px animal takes 2-4 s in numpy and a 10-px one well under a second.
   Fine for sprite sheets, not for a live renderer. The rig is small (about 40 tubes); a Rust
   port or pre-rendered sprite atlases per (species, pose, phase, yaw, size) would both work.

**What would help:** Ferrari-painted animals do not exist in the studio's references. A second
craft reference for small figures, such as hand-pixelled animals by any master at 8-60 px, would
let me copy a figure rather than a boulder. The naturalist's `elk_bull` and `elk_herd` photo
sheets were the most useful input in the whole study.

A fresh-eyes critic reviewed two rounds; their rankings drove stages 3-6 after the first draft.
